# C96: persistent outer gates with an exact transverse cost

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT, not a fact-graph result. Formal
verification is forbidden by the current allocation. The original target is
OPEN. This is the second and last allocated round (completed ceiling 27).

Accepted sources used: b98bb807d514e80d (single-cut and Huber bounds),
1fca3e5dd555cb4a (ray feasibility and objective), e9a55f57461f253c (the
stronger fixed H6 reference), and 04219554e41d9750 (the unconditional
original-denominator limit Z_1). No conditional seed from the last fact is
assumed. C93--C95 are motivation, never proof premises. All new assertions
used in this draft are proved together below.

## 1. Static model, physical word, and parameters

Fix an integer m>=6. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+1/m),
beta=1-1/m, a=beta/c, and ell=1+a. Define H_0=() and

    H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a*c^k,H_k), 0<=k<m.

Every subword uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Let

    j=2^(m-1)-1, n=2^m-1, P=sum H_(m-1),
    r=a*c^(m-1), b=1+r, d=1/(P+1), sigma=r*d/(2P+1),
    Kcut=r^2*d^2/(2P+1),
    p=12/13, q=5/13, rho=q^2/p^2=25/144,
    kappa=1/ell, J=1+rho*ell, w=1/(2p^2)=169/288.

The symbol Kcut is the accepted central single-cut value on this exact word;
it is not the entire worst-case functional W_n and need not equal the Huber
value. Choose dimensionless constants

    0<epsilon<=1/4, |eta|<=1/4.

For positive Rminus,Rplus define phi(0)=0 and
phi'(s)=clip(s,[-Rminus,Rplus]). Explicitly phi(s)=s^2/2 in the core,
phi(s)=Rplus*s-Rplus^2/2 on the positive outer interval, and
phi(s)=-Rminus*s-Rminus^2/2 on the negative outer interval.

Use u_+=(p,q), u_-=(p,-q), positive radii

    Rplus_+=p*d*(1+epsilon), Rplus_-=p*d*(1-epsilon),
    Rminus_+=p*sigma*(1+eta), Rminus_-=p*sigma*(1-eta),

and the corresponding two functions phi_+,phi_-. Define ONE static function

    F(x,y)=kappa*y^2/2+w*phi_+(p*x+q*y)+w*phi_-(p*x-q*y).

Run actual GD from X_0=(1,0) using L=1 and exactly the word H_m. There is
no objective switch, stepsize adaptation, or local curvature rescaling.

## 2. Global legality and the actual minimizer

The useful uniform parameter bounds are

    2/7<a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2, 9/7<ell<3/2,
    0<kappa<7/9, 0<rho<1/5, 1<J<13/10,
    P>31, r>9, b>10, b/r<10/9.                     (C96.1)

Indeed a=(m-1)/((m+1)*lambda)>=5/(7lambda)>2/7 because
lambda<5/2; also a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1. There are at least 31 steps in
H_(m-1), all greater than one. Finally c>2 gives
r>(2/7)*2^5=64/7>9. The remaining inequalities follow by substitution.

For any two scalar arguments, the difference of a clip is theta times
the difference of those arguments, where 0<=theta<=1. Thus the difference
of gradients at X,Y equals D(X-Y), where

    D=diag(0,kappa)+w*theta_+*u_+*u_+^T+w*theta_-*u_-*u_-^T,
    0<=D<=C=diag(1,kappa+rho)<=diag(1,137/144)<=I.

The Euclidean norm of D is at most one, proving global 1-smoothness.
Each summand is convex, proving convexity of F. Every summand is
nonnegative. Since the two nonparallel ridge vectors span R^2 and each
phi vanishes only at zero, argmin F={0} and min F=0. Consequently the
true initial squared distance is exactly one and the payoff is V=2F(X_n).

The quadratic background is singular, so it has no positive curvature
floor on the full active plane. The total core matrix C is not I. These
properties follow from exact algebraic inequalities, not rounded clipping.

## 3. Exact prefix and its central queried gate

Write X_i=(x_i,y_i). Put

    delta=epsilon*p*d, zeta=eta*p*sigma,
    y_plus=-epsilon*(q/p)*d*ell,
    y_minus=eta*(q/p)*sigma*ell,
    Lcut=epsilon*d*J/2.

As long as both ridges are in the positive outer intervals, the gradient is

    grad F=(d,kappa*y+delta*q/p^2).

The FIRST physical step is ell=1/kappa. It sends y_0=0 exactly to y_plus,
the stationary value of this affine scalar recursion. Every subsequent
step preserves y_plus until a ridge changes gate, irrespective of its size.
The proposed prefix is therefore

    x_i=1-d*sum_(t<i) h_t,
    y_0=0, y_i=y_plus for 1<=i<=j.                 (C96.2)

Here is an induction validating every proposed gate, rather than assuming
the affine formula remains applicable. At i=0 both projections are p, while
Rplus_+<=5p/(4(P+1))<p. For 1<=i<j, the remaining prefix mass includes its
last step ell, so x_i-d>=ell*d. Hence

    p*x_i+q*y_plus-Rplus_+
       >=p*d*(ell-epsilon*J)>0.

The other projection is even larger than p*x_i, and Rplus_-<p*d. This
proves that both ridges remain strictly positive outer before index j.
Thus (C96.2) follows from the ACTUAL recursion, and x_j=d.

At index j the first ridge projection is

    p*d+q*y_plus=p*d*(1-epsilon*(J-1)).

It is positive because epsilon*(J-1)<3/40, and strictly less than
Rplus_+. It therefore lies strictly in that ridge's core. The second
projection is p*d*(1+epsilon*(J-1))>Rplus_-, so the second ridge is still
positive outer. Direct differentiation now gives

    grad F(X_j)=(d-Lcut,-(q/p)*Lcut),
    x_(j+1)=-r*d+b*Lcut,
    y_(j+1)=epsilon*(q/p)*d*(b*J/2-ell).          (C96.3)

The last y-coordinate is positive by b>10 and ell<3/2. In particular the
central gradient is noncollinear with the stationary precentral gradient
(d,0). The actual gate Hessians diag(0,kappa) and
diag(0,kappa)+w*u_+*u_+^T do not commute: their off-diagonal commutator
has nonzero magnitude kappa*w*p*q.

## 4. Both ridges stay negative outer throughout the full suffix

First verify the point immediately after the central step. By (C96.1),

    x_(j+1)+(q/p)*abs(y_(j+1))
      <=d*(-r+b*epsilon*J*(1+rho)/2)
      <d*(-r+39*b/200)
      <-47*r*d/60.

Since 2P+1>63 and |eta|<=1/4, this is strictly below

    -sigma*(1+|eta|) >= -5*r*d/(4*(2P+1)).

More explicitly, 47/60>5/(4*(2P+1)), so the last strict comparison has
the required orientation. Both ridge projections are therefore below
their own negative thresholds at X_(j+1).

On the negative outer intersection the full gradient is

    grad F=(-sigma,kappa*y-q*zeta/p^2).

The FIRST step of the right H_(m-1) block is again ell. It sets y exactly
to y_minus. Subsequent steps preserve that value as long as the gates
stay negative outer, while x increases monotonically. The proposed terminal
point is

    x_n=-sigma*(P+1)+b*Lcut, y_n=y_minus.         (C96.4)

To validate all the proposed post-first-step suffix gates it suffices to
check the largest proposed x, namely x_n. The inequality

    x_n+(q/p)*abs(y_minus)<-sigma*(1+|eta|)

is equivalent to

    P-|eta|*J > (b/r)*(2P+1)*epsilon*J/2.        (C96.5)

The left side exceeds P-13/40. The right side is less than
13*(2P+1)/72. Their difference exceeds
(23/36)*P-13/40-13/72>0, because P>31. This proves (C96.5).
Induction now verifies the complete actual suffix, including its unqueried
terminal point. It does not replace physical steps by a Hessian-dependent
schedule. Every one of the slanted ridges remains strictly negative outer
at every postcentral query and at the terminal point.

This trajectory NEVER visits the common core intersection. In particular,
it refutes an unconditional extension of eventual common-core capture to
all singular-background, nonsaturated-core models. Its poor objective
cannot be attributed to entering the common quadratic region.

## 5. Exact objective and a whole two-parameter family obstruction

When both ridges are negative outer, expanding the defining static function
gives

    F(x,y)=-sigma*x-(q*zeta/p^2)*y
           -(sigma^2+zeta^2/p^2)/2+kappa*y^2/2.

No function-value offsets are deleted. At y=y_minus=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),
the transverse linear and quadratic terms combine to
-q^2*zeta^2/(2p^4*kappa). Substituting (C96.4) yields

    V=2F(X_n)
     =Kcut-2*sigma*b*Lcut-zeta^2*J/p^2
     =Kcut*(1-epsilon*b*J/r-eta^2*J/(2P+1)).    (C96.6)

This is the main joint statement: for EVERY m>=6 and EVERY allowed
epsilon,eta the single static two-ridge function above is globally legal,
has the stated actual minimizer and queried itinerary, and obeys (C96.6).
The proof is entirely symbolic and independent of finite trajectory runs.

The payoff is strictly smaller than Kcut because epsilon>0. V is positive,
since the terminal point is strictly in the negative outer region and is
not the unique minimizer. As an algebraic check, epsilon*b*J/r<13/36
and eta^2*J/(2P+1)<13/10080, so the displayed bracket is positive as well.

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives Kcut<=K_n(H_m), and accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m). Consequently
this entire explicit changing-region family fails to improve even the
accepted central single cut, for all horizons in its stated domain. A
negative tilt eta cannot repair the loss: it incurs the exact additional
quadratic price in (C96.6). This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

The same conclusion prices a free initial transverse displacement as long
as the initial point (1,y0) lies in both positive outer regions. Its first
step sends y0 to the same y_plus, so all later states and terminal values
are unchanged. Its TRUE initial squared distance is 1+y0^2; its normalized
payoff is the expression in (C96.6) divided by 1+y0^2. A large transverse
initial coordinate cannot be silently removed from the denominator.

## 6. A scalable law on the ORIGINAL enclosing-m sequence

Fix epsilon,eta in the displayed ranges independently of m. Let
M_k=1+sum H_k and r_k=a*c^k. From the physical word,

    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+r_k,
    M_k=2^k+a*(c^k-2^k)/(c-2).                 (C96.7)

Here c,a still depend on m. As m tends to infinity, c tends to lambda,
a tends to 1/lambda=lambda-2, (2/c)^(m-1) tends to zero, and therefore
M_(m-1)/c^(m-1) tends to 1. Also (1+1/m)^(m-1) tends to exp(1). Thus

    r/(P+1) -> 1/lambda,
    lambda^m*Kcut -> 1/(2*exp(1)*lambda),
    J -> 1+rho*sqrt(2), b/r -> 1.

The second limit follows directly by writing
Kcut=[r/(P+1)]^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). In (C96.6), the eta term tends to zero.
We obtain the fully priced nonzero limit

    lambda^m*V ->
      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda].   (C96.8)

The unconditional portion of accepted fact 04219554e41d9750 states that
Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) exists and is finite and positive, for the
ORIGINAL all-split benchmark denominator. Hence

    B_(2^m-1)*V ->
      Z_1*[1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/[2*exp(1)*lambda]. (C96.9)

Only that accepted unconditional limit is used; no seed assumption or
conditional separation from the same fact is imported. Formula (C96.9)
is the payoff of this exhibited family, not the true worst-case value.

For comparison with the same-word global Huber witness, S=sum H_m and
M_m=1+S. Equations (C96.7) give lambda^m/(1+2S)->1/(2*exp(1)), so

    V / [1/(1+2S)] ->
      [1-epsilon*(1+rho*sqrt(2))]/lambda < 1.

No finite m=6,7,8 observation is used to prove these limits. Conversely
none of those finite cases is described as a test of an m>=128 theorem.

## 7. Bounded discovery, reconstruction, and failure accounting

The round started from the existing ledger at 16 charged cases, 14 discovery
cases, one calibration, one exact reconstruction, and 0.6265509749991907
measured/accounted seconds. Calibration against the accepted scalar central
cut had already passed in case1; it was not rerun or treated as free.

Cases17--22 weakened or removed the vertical ridge in the previous rational
paired-ridge models. All six returned finite trajectories and changed queried
gates; none exceeded K. Cases23--28 replaced that vertical ridge by a singular
quadratic background with rational coefficient. All six also returned finite
trajectories and no gain. The largest successful new discovery ratio was
case23 at approximately 0.8972369014212274*K. Its background has a zero
horizontal eigenvalue, so no uniform positive background floor is presumed.

Case29 was a CHARGED FAILED proposal. Its exact algebraic-curvature prefilter
raised TypeError: "'>=' not supported between instances of 'Q2' and 'int'".
The raw error, request/source hashes, and public launch event are retained.
The run stopped after that error. Defining the missing exact comparison
operators repaired the implementation; separately charged case30 retried
the same proposed static function. No accounting was reset or erased.

Case30 uses epsilon=1/12, eta=0 in the family above. Case31 uses
epsilon=eta=1/12. Case32 uses epsilon=1/4, eta=0. Their numerical V/K ratios
were respectively approximately 0.8959721668863, 0.8959216103034, and
0.6879165006589. Their actual two-ridge itineraries are exactly the proposed
central chronology, with changes at indices 31,32 for ridge1 and 32 for
ridge2. Both terminal ridges are negative outer. No finite gain is claimed.

Broad discovery stopped after these cases. The final two reserves were used
ONLY for exact reconstruction of the SAME static objects as cases30 and31:
cases33 and34 respectively. They preserve the original m=6 physical steps,
algebraic kappa, rational directions and weights, all radii definitions, and
initial point. Exact Q(sqrt(2)) arithmetic used rational-square sign tests;
decimal conversion was only for readable outputs. Both runs checked every
actual gate, every sampled value/gradient/point, the central and terminal
coordinate formulas, and the EXACT identity (C96.6), including its strict
comparison with the central cut. Neither is a verifier verdict.

The cumulative ledger before closure has 34 charged cases: 30 discovery
(including the one charged error), one calibration, and three exact
reconstructions. There are 33 finite raw statuses and one error. The two
remaining discovery slots are retired on allocation closure; reserves are
fully used. No solver, installation, formal verifier call, C59 replay, or
benchmark dynamic program was run. Final metered seconds and hashes are
recorded in the companion manifest after closure, not estimated here.

## 8. Same-word references and interpretation

All forward results retain the global Huber term, every single-cut value,
the best single-cut index, and the sufficient first-two-ray comparison on
the SAME physical word. The complete finite symbolic argument showing
F_ray=K for m=6,7,8 is reproduced as an integrated appendix below. It is
joint draft mathematics, not an invocation of unverified C95 as a fact.

For m=6 the stronger accepted reference is e9a55f57461f253c:
W_63(H6)>56/2711>1/50. The exhibited root family is far smaller even by
an elementary comparison. If t_k=r_k/M_k, then
t_(k+1)=c*t_k/(2+t_k). Since c>lambda, one has
c*(c-2)>lambda*(lambda-2)=1>beta and hence a<c-2. Also
c<=7*lambda/6<35/12<3. Thus a<c-2<1, and induction gives t_k<c-2<1,
so Kcut<1/(2P+1)<1/63<1/50. The C59 reference is not transplanted to
different enclosing-m words, and none of its 4160 checks was replayed.

This work supplies a concrete scoped obstruction and a useful exact relation.
Persistent outer gates, a singular background, a nonsaturated core matrix,
and a noncommuting central gate do not by themselves yield improved payoff.
The decisive restriction in the explicit solved family is the reciprocal
short-step coefficient, which kills the transverse transient at the first
step of each child. General coefficients retain a transverse polynomial
and are outside this exact family theorem. General multilevel gate changes,
unequal ridge directions/weights, and arbitrary static models remain open.

The general coefficient relation, useful for a future authorized round, is
as follows. Retain a fixed kappa>0 and the same symmetric directions/weights
and radii means. Suppose the ACTUAL chronology is both plus before the
central query, core/plus there, and both minus afterwards. Let
R=product_(h in H_(m-1))(1-kappa*h), delta=(Rplus_+-Rplus_-)/2,
zeta=(Rminus_+-Rminus_-)/2, and

    yj=-delta*q*(1-R)/(p^2*kappa),
    L=(delta-q*yj)/(2p),
    yc=[1-b*(kappa+rho/2)]*yj-b*delta*q/(2p^2),
    ye=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),
    xn=-r*d+b*L+P*sigma, yn=ye+(yc-ye)*R.

Solving the two affine recursions and differentiating the central core gate
gives these formulas directly. Substitution into the exact static value gives

    V=-2*sigma*xn-2*q*zeta*yn/p^2-sigma^2-zeta^2/p^2+kappa*yn^2.

Unlike (C96.6), this last interface is CONDITIONAL on its stated complete
gate itinerary. It is not a construction certificate for arbitrary kappa.
The exact root family proves that itinerary separately in Sections 3--4.

The original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile problem remains unresolved.
No lower witness on H_m, even if stronger, would alone disprove minimax
optimality: that negative goal needs an improving schedule's full-class
upper certificate.

## Appendix: complete same-word finite scalar comparison

This appendix is included in the present joint derivation. It has no
unverified predecessor. Here K is the accepted functional K_n(H_m), and
F_ray is the complete accepted family from fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a.

Here is a direct sufficient test for the complete accepted ray family to
equal K. For i<j put P=sum_(t<i)h_t and D=sum_(i<t<j)h_t. If

    (h_i-1)(h_j-1)<(1+P)(1+D)                 (R)

for every i<j, no feasible accepted ray selection has two checkpoints.
Indeed its first two checkpoint gradients are
m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=(h_i-1)m_0/(1+D), with old offset P*m_0.
The second-return budget is (h_j-1)m_1-P*m_0<m_0 by (R).
With exactly two checkpoints the old-gradient requirement is at least
m_0, and with more checkpoints the third magnitude would be below m_0.
Both contradict the explicit feasibility conditions of 1fca3e5dd555cb4a.
Zero and one checkpoint produce exactly K. Any numerical check of (R)
will be labeled numerical until an exact symbolic bound is supplied.



Write r_j=a*c^j and M_j=1+sum H_j. Then

    M_0=1, M_(j+1)=2*M_j+r_j,
    t_j=r_j/M_j, t_0=a, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).

For m>=6, c>1+sqrt(2) and beta<1 imply a<c-2. Hence t_j increases
and stays below c-2. Also

    beta-(c-2) = 3-(1+sqrt(2))-(2+sqrt(2))/m
       >= (10-7*sqrt(2))/6 >0,

so t_j>=a>(c-2)/c.

For any two chronological indices i<j with levels u,v, define the ratio
R_ij in (R) with the right side as its denominator. If u>=v, the prefix
before i contains H_u and the gap before j contains H_v; thus
R_ij<=t_u*t_v. The largest level m-1 occurs only once, so at least one
of u,v is at most m-2. It follows that

    R_ij<=t_(m-1)*t_(m-2)<c*t_(m-2)^2.

If u<v, both the prefix before i and the gap from i to j contain H_u.
Their total mass is at least sum H_v-(1+r_u), since the prefix before
j contains H_v. Consequently, putting x=1+P and y=1+D,
x,y>=M_u and x+y>=M_v-r_u. From
(x-M_u)(y-M_u)>=0 we obtain

    x*y>=M_u*(M_v-M_u-r_u).

Let d=v-u>=1 and t=t_u. Directly iterating the recurrence for M gives

    (M_v-M_u-r_u)/M_u
      =2^d-1+t*((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1)
      >=c^(d-1)+(c-1)*(2^d-2)/c>=c^(d-1).

The first inequality uses t>=(c-2)/c and the nonnegative coefficient
((c^d-2^d)/(c-2)-1). Therefore R_ij<=c*t_u^2<=c*t_(m-2)^2.
Equality in the complete maximum occurs at the first level-(m-2) bridge
and the immediately following level-(m-1) bridge: both intervening
prefix/gap masses are sum H_(m-2). Thus

    max_(i<j) R_ij = c*t_(m-2)^2.                         (A1)

This proves the full pair test symbolically; it does not rely on sampled
pair scans. Here are short rational upper certificates for the three
authorized horizons. Monotonicity of c*t/(2+t) in both positive variables
allows use of the displayed upper parameters throughout.

* m=6: c<17/6 and a<3/10. Four recurrence steps give
  t_4<83521/144462<7/12. Thus c*t_4^2<833/864<1.
  The intermediate bounds are 17/46,289/654,4913/9582,83521/144462.
* m=7: c<83/30 and a<5/16. Bounds for t_1,...,t_5 are respectively
  3/8,7/16,1/2,83/150,3/5. For the last step,
  6889/11490<3/5; hence c*t_5^2<747/750<1.
* m=8: c<68/25 and a<13/40. The upper recurrence has reciprocal at
  its sixth step equal to
  25/18+(395/234)*(25/34)^6. This is greater than 33/20 because
  (25/34)^3>79/200 and (79/200)^2>611/3950. Therefore t_6<20/33,
  and c*t_6^2<27200/27225<1.

All parameter enclosures follow by squaring positive rational bounds for
sqrt(2). In particular sqrt(2)<10/7, sqrt(2)>29/21 prove the m=6
bounds; sqrt(2)<341/240 and sqrt(2)>7/5 prove m=7;
sqrt(2)<319/225 and sqrt(2)>163/117 prove m=8.
Combining (A1) with the accepted ray feasibility conditions proves

    F_ray_(2^m-1)(H_m)=K_(2^m-1)(H_m), m=6,7,8.           (A2)

No m>=128 result is used here. This finite symbolic comparison is part of the present UNVERIFIED joint
draft; it is not an accepted graph fact.

For completeness the largest single-cut term is the central cut. For a
fixed level u put p=1+P, q=1+T; both are at least M_u and
p+q=M_m-r_u. The denominator p^2*(2q-1) has no interior minimum on
that interval: its derivative is 2p*(2*(M_m-r_u)-3p-1), with at most
one interior critical point, a maximum. The two endpoint denominators
are ordered by monotonicity of z^2/(2z-1) for z>=1, so the earliest
occurrence of level u is best. Its value is

    t_u^2/[2*M_m-2*M_u-2*r_u-1].

The numerator increases and the positive denominator decreases with u.
Thus the central level m-1 maximizes it. In particular K is exactly
the larger of the global Huber term and the central single-cut term;
no choice of untested checkpoints is hidden in the numerical reference.

# max3 round27 handoff — C96

This is the second and last newly authorized round. Completed baseline25;
completed26 before this round; this return completes27, the authorized
ceiling. The attempted ceiling including the historical interruption is28.
Runtime currently reports running/round2; no observed max_rounds state is
claimed before returning. No round or numerical renewal is inferred.

Original target: OPEN. New accepted facts:0. Formal verifier calls:0.
Latest master_guidance re-read:6e0a18d700dcd62f, unchanged.

The COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED C96 draft is finding2d8525da4bc3b684,
public file research/max3_c96_persistent_outer_gate_exact_loss.md.
Counterexample a18eedfbe5d827cf records the failure of unconditional
common-core capture. Scoped closure cc5db6e6597542fc prices only the
displayed family. The precursor plan is2032a0fd3a8973ab.

For every original H_m,m>=6, define ell=1+a, P=sum H_(m-1), central
step b, d=1/(P+1), sigma=(b-1)*d/(2P+1), p=12/13,q=5/13,
kappa=1/ell, J=1+(25/144)*ell. The STATIC function is

    kappa*y^2/2+(169/288)*[phi_+(p*x+q*y)+phi_-(p*x-q*y)].

Its positive radii are p*d*(1+/-epsilon), and its negative radii are
p*sigma*(1+/-eta), where0<epsilon<=1/4 and|eta|<=1/4. Its total core
matrix is diag(1,kappa+25/144)<=diag(1,137/144), its background is
singular, and its unique minimizer is0. Starting at(1,0), its true

## DOC-002619 — project/research/max3_chebyshev_cut_obstruction.md

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002619 -->
# An explicit ordering defeating quadratics plus all single-cut Huber witnesses

Status: complete candidate derivation, unverified. The initial-wave verification
allowance is already used by fact b98bb807d514e80d. This strengthening is for
main-agent prioritization. Worker: max3.

## Statement and scope

Let K_n(h) be the verified single-cut functional in fact b98bb807d514e80d.
For a schedule h define its exact quadratic-class worst case

\[
Q_n(h)=\max_{0\leq\lambda\leq1}
\lambda\prod_{t=0}^{n-1}(1-h_t\lambda)^2,
\qquad J_n(h)=\max\{K_n(h),Q_n(h)\}.
\]

Here Q_n is exactly the normalized worst case over convex quadratics in any
finite dimension with gradient Lipschitz constant at most L, after scaling
L=1. Indeed diagonalizing their symmetric Hessian makes the normalized error
a weighted average of the displayed scalar expression, and each scalar value
is attained by a one-dimensional quadratic. Thus J_n(h)<=W_n(h).

For every integer n>=3 put theta=pi/(2n+1) and

\[
a_k=\frac1{\sin^2(k\theta)}\quad(1\leq k\leq n).
\]

Define h^(n) by listing a_k with even indices k in decreasing index order,
followed by a_k with odd indices k in increasing index order. For example the
index order at n=7 is (6,4,2,1,3,5,7). This is a fully explicit positive,
finite, predetermined schedule. Then

\[
Q_n(h^{(n)})=\frac1{(2n+1)^2},\qquad
K_n(h^{(n)})\leq72\sin^2\theta
\leq\frac{72\pi^2}{(2n+1)^2}.
\]

Using B_n=1/U_n^ZJ<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2 from verified fact
b98bb807d514e80d, it follows that

\[
\frac{J_n(h^{(n)})}{U_n^{ZJ}}
\leq\frac{72\pi^2(1+4n^p)}{(2n+1)^2}\longrightarrow0.
\]

This rules out proving the target by taking only the maximum of all quadratic
witnesses, the global Huber witness, and the single-cut witnesses in K_n.
It does not give any upper bound on W_n(h^(n)); a nonlinear history-dependent
witness could yield a much larger error.

## Exact quadratic calculation

Define polynomials T_0(x)=1, T_1(x)=x and
T_{r+1}(x)=2xT_r(x)-T_{r-1}(x) for integers r>=1. The cosine addition formula
and induction give T_r(cos u)=cos(ru). The recurrence also shows that T_r
has degree r and parity r. Write M=2n+1. Then T_M is odd and
T_M'(0)=M sin(M pi/2)=(-1)^n M, by differentiating the cosine identity
on -1<x<1. Therefore

\[
R_n(\lambda)=\frac{(-1)^nT_M(\sqrt\lambda)}{M\sqrt\lambda}
\]

extends to a polynomial of degree n with R_n(0)=1. For each k=1,...,n,
T_M(sin(k theta))=cos(M pi/2-k pi)=0. These give n distinct positive
roots of R_n, hence

\[
R_n(\lambda)=\prod_{k=1}^n\left(1-\frac\lambda{\sin^2(k\theta)}\right).
\]

For lambda in [0,1], |T_M(sqrt(lambda))|<=1. It follows that
lambda R_n(lambda)^2<=1/M^2. At lambda=1, T_M(1)=1 and equality holds.
Permutation of the step factors leaves the product unchanged, proving Q_n.

## Bounds on neighboring step sizes in the stated ordering

All a_k are at least1 and decrease with k. The function sin u/u decreases
on 0<u<=pi/2, since its derivative has numerator u cos u-sin u and the
derivative of that numerator is -u sin u<=0. Hence whenever k+2<=n,

\[
\frac{a_k}{a_{k+2}}
=\frac{\sin^2((k+2)\theta)}{\sin^2(k\theta)}
\leq\left(\frac{k+2}{k}\right)^2.
\tag{1}
\]

The first even-indexed step and the last odd-indexed step have index at
least n-1. Since n>=3 implies (n-1)/(2n+1)>=1/4, both are at most2.
Also a_2>=a_1/4 and a_3>=a_1/9, by the same decreasing-sinc argument
(or the double-angle identity for a_2).

## Every single-cut term remains small

For a selected position j, write b=h_j, P=P_j, T=T_j as in the verified
definition of K_n. Replace (b-1)_+ by b when an upper estimate is needed.

1. A step before the central largest step a_1 has even index. If it is the
first step, b/(P+1)<=2. Otherwise its preceding step is a_{k+2}; by (1),
b<=4a_{k+2}<=4P. Thus b/(P+1)<=4 in either case. Its suffix contains a_1,
so its cut term is at most16/(1+2a_1)<=8/a_1.

2. At a_1 the prefix contains a_2>=a_1/4 and the suffix contains
a_3>=a_1/9. Therefore its cut term is at most

\[
\frac{a_1^2}{(a_1/4)^2(2a_1/9)}=\frac{72}{a_1}.
\]

3. After a_1, the prefix always has P>=a_1. For a step a_k that is not
the last step, k is odd and k>=3, and its suffix contains a_{k+2}.
By (1), a_{k+2}>=a_k/4. Its cut term is at most

\[
\frac{a_k^2}{a_1^2(2a_k/4)}
=\frac{2a_k}{a_1^2}\leq\frac2{a_1}.
\]

At the last step b<=2, hence (b-1)^2<=1 and the cut term is at most
1/a_1^2<=1/a_1.

Finally the global Huber term is at most1/(1+2a_1)<=1/(2a_1).
The maximum of these bounds is72/a_1=72 sin^2 theta. Since sin(theta)<=theta,
this proves the claimed estimate, entirely analytically for every n>=3.

## Source grounding

The standard trigonometric definition of T_r is documented at NIST DLMF
18.5.1, https://dlmf.nist.gov/18.5. The polynomial identities used here are
derived above from their recurrence, so no external theorem is used as a
black-box proof step. Matlas was queried for the weighted Chebyshev product
identity and did not return a useful exact hit.

## A legal two-curvature witness exposes the missing interaction

The schedules above are severely unstable on an explicit globally legal
one-dimensional function. This calculation is part of the same unverified
candidate package and does not claim that every schedule is unstable.

For n>=3 keep M=2n+1 and theta=pi/M and put
mu=sin^2(5 theta/2). Define

\[
f_n(x)=\begin{cases}
\mu x^2/2,&x\geq0,\\
x^2/2,&x\leq0.
\end{cases}
\]

The derivative equals mu x on the nonnegative side and x on the nonpositive
side. It is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz: within each half-line
this holds by its slope, while for x<=0<=y its increment mu y-x is between0
and y-x. Hence f_n is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and minimized at0.
Start x_0=1.

The chosen angle gives sin^2(2theta)<mu<sin^2(3theta). Along the even-index
prefix, every index k>=4 has 1-a_k mu>0, so the iterates stay positive.
At the last even step a_2, the factor is negative, so the next point is
negative. The following step a_1 therefore uses curvature1; because a_1>1,
it changes the sign back to positive. Every remaining odd-index step has
k>=3 and 1-a_k mu>0, so all later iterates stay positive. No factor is zero.

Relative to the ordinary quadratic product R_n(mu), exactly one factor,
at a_1, is replaced. Consequently

\[
x_n=\frac{1-a_1}{1-a_1\mu}R_n(\mu).
\]

Since arcsin(sqrt(mu))=5theta/2, the trigonometric identity proved above gives
R_n(mu)=sin(5pi/2)/(M sqrt(mu))=1/(M sqrt(mu)). Thus the exact normalized
error on this legal instance is

\[
2 f_n(x_n)=\mu x_n^2
=\frac{\cos^4\theta}
{M^2[\sin^2(5\theta/2)-\sin^2\theta]^2}.
\tag{3}
\]

For every n>=3, theta<=pi/7<pi/4, so cos^4(theta)>=1/4, and the square
bracket in (3) is positive and at most(25/4)theta^2. Therefore

\[
W_n(h^{(n)})\geq\frac{4(2n+1)^2}{625\pi^4}.
\]

More precisely the displayed instance's error divided by M^2 tends to
16/(441 pi^4), since sin u/u tends to1 and cos u tends to1. This is a direct
analytic GD trajectory, not an interpolation relaxation or numerical test.

The mechanism is the ordered pair a_2,a_1: the first crosses into the unit
curvature side, the second amplifies there and returns to the low-curvature
side. A next all-schedule candidate can therefore retain a fixed pair of
one-sided curvatures and its chronological sign transition rule, rather than
only the terminal quadratic polynomial or one selected crossing.

# Exact two-envelope interpolation state across a GD cut

Status: support derivation, unverified; not a target proof or a proposed scalar
comparison with the Zhang--Jiang benchmark. Worker: max3.

Normalize L=1 and the minimizer datum to (x_star,g_star,f_star)=(0,0,0).
For every sampled jet (x_i,g_i,f_i) in a finite history I define

\[
y_i=x_i-g_i,\qquad z_i=f_i-\tfrac12\|g_i\|^2.
\]

The smooth-convex interpolation inequality is exactly

\[
z_i\geq z_j+\langle g_j,y_i-y_j\rangle\quad(i,j\in I).
\]

Define two finite polyhedral envelopes, retaining their full functions:

\[
A_I(u)=\max_{i\in I}\{z_i+\langle g_i,u-y_i\rangle\},
\qquad
B_I(v)=\max_{i\in I}\{\langle v,y_i\rangle-z_i\}.
\]

Both are nonnegative because the minimizer datum is in I. A proposed new jet
at x with gradient g and transformed value z=f-||g||^2/2 is compatible with
every old jet if and only if, writing y=x-g,

\[
A_I(y)\leq z\leq\langle g,y\rangle-B_I(g).
\tag{1}
\]

The left inequality lists all old-to-new constraints. The right inequality
lists all new-to-old constraints. In particular the exact gradient admissibility
test is A_I(x-g)+B_I(g)<=<g,x-g>. There is no maximization or deletion of
cross-correlations before this test.

After accepting (y,g,z), the updates are exactly

\[
A_{I+}(u)=\max\{A_I(u),z+\langle g,u-y\rangle\},\quad
B_{I+}(v)=\max\{B_I(v),\langle v,y\rangle-z\}.
\tag{2}
\]

Thus a full-history state can be represented by two polyhedral functions and
the current point/gradient. A redundant affine piece may be removed only when
it is pointwise dominated in the corresponding entire envelope. The number of
pieces is not bounded independently of the horizon by this derivation.

## Explicit legal witness for any admitted finite transcript

Let phi=A_I for the complete admitted transcript. Its defining inequalities
give phi(y_i)=z_i and
phi(u)>=phi(y_i)+<g_i,u-y_i>. Moreover phi>=0 and phi(0)=0.
Define the globally finite function

\[
f(x)=\min_u\{\phi(u)+\tfrac12\|x-u\|^2\}.
\tag{3}
\]

The minimizer p(x) exists because phi>=0 makes the objective coercive, and is
unique because the quadratic is strictly convex. At x_i, the displayed support
inequality and x_i=y_i+g_i show that the difference between the objective at u
and at y_i is at least ||u-y_i||^2/2. Therefore p(x_i)=y_i,
f(x_i)=z_i+||g_i||^2/2=f_i.

For completeness the smoothness argument is elementary. If p=p(x), minimizing
along the segment from p to an arbitrary u and using convexity of phi gives
phi(u)>=phi(p)+<x-p,u-p>. Thus x-p is a subgradient of phi at p. Applying
this support inequality in both directions at x and x' gives

\[
\langle (x-p(x))-(x'-p(x')),p(x)-p(x')\rangle\geq0.
\]

For r(x)=x-p(x), substitute p(x)-p(x')=x-x'-(r(x)-r(x')) to obtain
||r(x)-r(x')||^2<=<r(x)-r(x'),x-x'>. Cauchy--Schwarz gives that r is
1-Lipschitz. Evaluating (3) at p(x) proves
f(x+d)<=f(x)+<r(x),d>+||d||^2/2. Reversing the points and using the
Lipschitz bound on r gives a matching first-order lower estimate with error
at most 3||d||^2/2. Hence f is differentiable and grad f=r. Convexity follows
by taking convex combinations of candidate minimizing points in the jointly
convex objective of (3). Since phi>=0 and phi(0)=0, f>=0 and f(0)=0.
This proves finite-dimensional realizability, convexity, smoothness, and a valid
minimizer, with no appeal to a merely PSD matrix.

## Relation to the true objective, and its limitation

At step t, query at the point x_t already fixed by the prior GD update; choose
(g_t,z_t) satisfying (1), update the envelopes by (2), and set
x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t g_t. Starting from A=B=0 (the minimizer datum), every complete
admitted transcript is realized by (3), and every legal trajectory satisfies
(1). The final normalized objective is
2 z_n+||g_n||^2 when ||x_0||=1. Consequently taking the supremum of this
quantity over the exact envelope recursion and all finite dimensions is the
actual W_n(h). Zero steps and arbitrary finite positive steps need no exception.

Necessity of the interpolation inequality can also be derived directly: for
fixed x_j put q(x)=f(x)-<g_j,x>. This convex 1-smooth function is minimized
at x_j. The descent estimate, obtained by integrating its Lipschitz gradient,
gives q(x_i-grad q(x_i))<=q(x_i)-||grad q(x_i)||^2/2. Comparing with q(x_j)
is exactly the required inequality.

This is an exact growing-state reformulation, not a reduction in minimax
strength. No invariant here compares its value to U_n^ZJ. A claim that the
envelope game uniformly dominates that benchmark, without a new mechanism,
would merely restate the target lower bound and must be marked BLOCKED.

Source context: Taylor, Hendrickx, Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 1,
Theorem 4, Corollary 1 and their proofs were downloaded and read. The algebraic
state and witness proof above are written out to avoid using them as unproved
black boxes. No external theorem is invoked in this support derivation.

# C80: an exact two-dimensional four-step primitive obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint candidate, no verifier allocation. This tests a
specific full primitive interface, not the original asymptotic theorem.
The defining construction and every required normal are given below.

Let h=(a,b,c,d), with a,d>=0, b>1, c>1. Define

 P=a+1, B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, U=b+c+d, A=1+a+b+c+d.

Assume D>BC and put

 Kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC), t=B^2 C/Kappa.

Then Kappa>0 and0<t<1. In the Euclidean plane define

 u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))), v=-(B u+D w)/c,
 K=conv(u,v,w), F(x)=max_(g in K)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2).

Here K is a nondegenerate triangle. Since B u+c v+D w=0 and B,c,D>0,
zero is in its interior. Completing the square shows grad F=P_K,
the Euclidean projection onto K. For completeness, the projection normal
inequalities at two points imply
||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, so the projection is1-Lipschitz.
The unique-maximizer formula gives
0<=F(x+z)-F(x)-<P_K(x),z><=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2,
proving differentiability. F is convex as a supremum of affine functions.
Since0 is interior to K, for every x!=0 sufficiently small epsilon>0
has epsilon x in K, whence F(x)>=epsilon(1-epsilon/2)||x||^2>0.
Thus0 is its unique minimizer and F(0)=0.

The actual iterates and gradients are

 x=(P u,u,-B u,D w,w), g=(u,u,v,w,w).                    (1)

The four GD updates follow from c v=-B u-D w and D=d+1.
It remains to check that each listed gradient is the actual projection.
The inner products are

 ||u||^2=1, <u,w>=||w||^2=t,
 <u,v>=-(B+D t)/c, <v,w>=-(B+D)t/c.

At x0 the residual x0-u=a u has nonpositive pairing with v-u and w-u;
at x1 the residual is zero. At x2 the residual y2=-B u-v satisfies

 <w-v,y2>=[-B^2(c-1)+Kappa t]/c^2=0,
 <u-v,y2>=-B(1-t)(c-1)/c<0.

The first equality uses the expansion
Kappa=D(2B+D)+c(D-BD+B)-B c^2
       =B^2(c-1)+(b+c+d)(D-B(c-1)).
At x3 the residual d w pairs to zero with u-w and to
-d t[1+(B+D)/c]<=0 with v-w. At x4 the residual is zero.
These are all vertex comparisons and establish every projection normal.

Put f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Then for every original ordered pair

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
      =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.

The optimum comparisons hold because0 belongs to K. In fact Q_(i,star)=0
for all i. There is one explicit globally legal function, with true initial
distance P to its unique minimizer, and no missing terminal comparison.

For the intrinsic energy

 V_h=2sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f_4)+sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                            -s(s+1)||g_4||^2,

where s=A-1, summing the GD norm identities and using
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives

 V_h=||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2
     =P^2-A^2 t.                                       (2)

Any original full nonnegative interpolation/PSD certificate must therefore
obey the following additional necessary inequality in this regime:

 P^2 D >= BC[P^2+2BP+BU].                               (3)

Indeed A=P+U and
A^2 B^2C-P^2 Kappa=U{BC[P^2+2BP+BU]-P^2D}, so (2)>=0
is equivalent to (3). This is only a necessary condition for b,c>1 and
D>BC; it is not a full characterization or a global capacity obstruction.

## Fixed rational specialization

One proposed exact word, selected by rounding the geometry of saved
discovery record71, is

 h=(1450,2269,1656,1345)/1000, sum h=168/25.              (4)

The rational evaluator is a single charged fixed-candidate operation.
It will preserve every original rational Gram entry, function value and
ordered Q, all four cut tests, the full scalar-curvature polynomial,
and the exact energy. No numerical PSD repair, clipping, solver rank
assumption or scan is used to establish the triangle construction.

# C79: four-step full-cone discovery and exact interfaces

Status: UNVERIFIED joint research draft. Wave8, cumulative round18.
No fact_submit is authorized. All new algebra below is derived jointly;
older shared drafts are orientation, never correctness premises.
The accepted interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290 is the only fact used.

## Primitive energy and the full cone

Normalize smoothness to1. For a nonnegative word h of length n, put
s=sum h_i, A=s+1, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, T_i=sum_(t>i)h_t and
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. Define

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Let Lambda be the full nonnegative array on distinct finite indices0..n,
with zero diagonal. Set e=(1,...,1)^T, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i, and

 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).

Expansion of the value coefficients gives L^T e=omega, and expansion of
the gradient coefficients gives

 V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>,
 M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.                                      (1)

For example the position part of sum Lambda Q has matrix
sym(L(T-I)); the squared-gradient differences contribute
sym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding the displayed diagonal of V gives (1).
Consequently the original full interface is exactly flow, Lambda>=0 and
M>=0. It is sufficient for V>=0 on every actual run, by Q_ij<=0 from
accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Every original finite gradient is retained.

Flow alone forces e^T M e=0: Te has entries1+P_i (P_n=s), and
e^T LTe=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2, whereas
e^T De=-s^2-sum h_i^2. PSD therefore forces Me=0. Indeed for any v,
positivity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs of small t forces
v^T Me=0. Thus the exact dual formulation may impose Me=0 and maximize
t subject to M-t(I-ee^T/(n+1))>=0. Its t>=0 feasibility is precisely the
original cone. This removes only the proved constant-gradient kernel;
no quadratic equality, rank, ordering, symmetry or sparsity is assumed.

The companion full primal minimizes V subject to Gram(g)>=0,
trace(Gram(g))=1, f_n=0 and every original ordered Q<=0. Its value is
always <=0: a constant-gradient Huber run gives V=0. Every negative
feasible transcript can be lifted to a legal function with a minimizer.
To see this, append the coordinate
g'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,R-epsilon P_i),
f'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 P_i, with epsilon>0. Then Q'_ij=Q_ij.
Choose finite c and R so

 c>=max_i(-f_i+epsilon^2 P_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2),
 epsilon R>=max_i(f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2).

These inequalities add the zero optimum jet in both orientations. The
conjugate construction of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the actual
convex1-smooth function. The additional V is epsilon^2 times
2sum h_i(s-P_i)+sum h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0, since
2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2. No distance-preservation claim is made.

## Zero steps: exact two-way cone correspondence

Partition the query indices into maximal consecutive classes separated
by positive steps. In each class except the last, choose the index of
the positive outgoing step as representative; choose n in the last.
Let hhat be the ordered list of positive steps. Embedding any compressed
certificate on these representatives gives the original V and Q on
their independent formal gradients. All omitted coefficients are zero,
so its full matrix is the coordinate-selection congruence of the
compressed PSD matrix. This proves the forward embedding.

Conversely, restrict an original certificate to formal gradients and
values that are equal within each class. Its V becomes V_hhat; each
cross-class Q becomes the corresponding compressed Q; each within-class
Q is zero. Sum all original nonnegative multipliers on each ordered
pair of distinct classes. This yields compressed nonnegative multipliers,
the compressed flow, and a PSD matrix by congruence. Thus certificate
existence is equivalent in both directions, including all-zero schedules
(V=0, Lambda=0). No numerical model below uses this compression: all
five original gradients are still saved and optimized.

## Necessary filters proved from legal functions

For j with b=h_j>1, write P=P_j, R=T_j and a=(b-1)/(R+1)>0.
Use the one-dimensional convex1-smooth function whose derivative is
clip(x,-a,1), with minimum0, and start at x_0=P+1. Every query through
j is on the upper plateau, the bridge lands at -(R+1)a, and all later
queries remain on the lower plateau until x_n=-a. At every query
f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, equivalently Q_(i,star)=0. Summing the GD norm
identity and substituting this equality into V yields

 V_h=(P+1)^2-A^2 a^2.

Thus a necessary condition for the cone is

 A(h_j-1)_+ <= (P_j+1)(T_j+1), for every j.                (2)

For h_j<=1 this is automatic. At j=0 or n-1, (2) gives
h_j<=2(s+1)/(s+2)<2 when h_j>1. Also, for h_j>1,
h_j-1 <=(P_j+1)(T_j+1)/A<=min(P_j+1,T_j+1), because
A=P_j+T_j+h_j+1 >=max(P_j+1,T_j+1).
At n4 this bounds h_1,h_2<4 using the adjacent endpoint bound.
So [0,2]x[0,4]x[0,4]x[0,2] is a lossless closed outer box.
It does not imply all points of the box, or all points satisfying (2),
are primitive. In particular no upper sum below12 is claimed.

For curvature z in(0,1], use f(x)=z x^2/2, x_0=1, and
p_0(z)=1, p_(i+1)(z)=(1-z h_i)p_i(z). Direct substitution gives

 V_h(z)=sum_i z h_i(1+z-z h_i)p_i(z)^2
                      -s z[1+(s+1)z]p_n(z)^2 >=0.        (3)

At z=1 this becomes 1-A^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2>=0 by telescoping.
Each fixed z gives a necessary test only. The implementation records
z=1,1/2,1/4; it performs no hidden curvature or word scan.

## Exact calibrations

The word (1,2,1,1) is the ConPP join of (1) and (1,1): their masses are
2 and3, the bridge increment d=1 solves d(2+3+d)=2*3, and the physical
bridge is1+d=2. Independently of any external composition theorem, its
complete nonzero certificate entries are

 01:2, 10:4, 21:3, 31:3, 41:3, 14:3,
 23:8, 32:16, 34:35, 43:48, 24:3.

Substitution in (1) gives exactly
M=(e_0-e_1)(e_0-e_1)^T+4(e_2-e_3)(e_2-e_3)^T
                               +16(e_3-e_4)(e_3-e_4)^T.
The child certificates are lambda01=2,lambda10=4,M=(e0-e1)^2
for (1), and lambda01=2,lambda10=4,lambda12=8,lambda21=12,
M=(e0-e1)^2+4(e1-e2)^2 for (1,1).
Thus the exact primitive primal minimum is0, attained by constant jets.

For (3,1,1,1), use x=(1,-2,-3/2,-1,-1/2),
g=(1,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2), f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.
These are samples of the same clipped-gradient function with slopes
[-1/2,1]. They give V=-45/4 and sum g_i^2=2, hence the full primal
minimum is at most -45/8. All20 original interpolation inequalities
and both optimum orientations are obeyed. This fixes the expected sign.

An additional exact length4 zero-remainder ConPP calibration is built
recursively from the empty word: join children a,b of masses A,C using
d>0 satisfying d(A+C+d)=AC, k=((A+C+d)/C)^2, and h=(a,1+d,b).
For bridge query p and terminal query z, the exact identity is

 V_h=V_a+k V_b-(k-1)[sum_(j in b nonterminal)b_j Q_(j,p)
                 +Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)].  (4)

It follows by expanding Q and using (k-1)(C-d)=2(A+d),
k d^2=A^2 and k C^2=(A+C+d)^2; C>d>0 gives every actual sign.
Each recursive child retains all its original indices. The baseline
uses a=join(empty,empty) and b=join(empty,a), then join(a,b).
The primitive calibration is (1), (4) and its explicit multiplier array,
not an ordinary objective-gap claim. Source terminology was checked in
Zhang--Jiang, arXiv2410.12395v2, Definition2.7 and Theorem3.1 proof;
the displayed identities are independently rederived here.

## Numerical scope and accounting

The authorized new ledger is max3_four_step_seed_discovery_budget.json:
128 charged model/optimization evaluations,120 measured build-plus-solve
seconds,96 distinct nonbaseline proposals,n4 only. Every proposal is
registered before its evaluation, including filtered or failed proposals;
all original data and source hashes are saved. Old closed ledgers are
neither imported as executable runners nor modified.

The search uses all four physical step coordinates with s>=168/25,
the lossless box, necessary (2)-(3), and full primal/dual sensitivities.
Several distant initializations are explored adaptively; neither the sum
nor the rank nor the order is fixed as a global reduction. A local
non-discovery is not global nonexistence. Numerical near-zero, success
status, a clipped eigenvalue or PSD repair is never an exact seed.
Any actual result must satisfy the original flow, all20 signs, full PSD
and sum threshold exactly; C78's separate audit must also be checked.

## DOC-002623 — project/research/max3_general_homogeneous_flow_obstruction.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\max3_general_homogeneous_flow_obstruction.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002623 -->
# Arbitrary even profiles with discrete radial homogeneity are too weak on H_m

Status: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No verifier
call. This extends the fixed-staircase analysis to every shape within a radial
period. Evenness and the stated exact discrete homogeneity are essential
hypotheses; the full scalar and multidimensional classes remain unresolved.

## 1. Statement

Let c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, and

    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    N_m=2^m-1,
    s_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

Consider ANY nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz function g:[0,infinity)->[0,infinity)
with g(0)=0 and

    g(cR)=c g(R),                         R>=0.              (1)

Extend it oddly to the real line and set
f(x)=integral_0^|x| g(R)dR. This gives an even convex differentiable
1-smooth function with fixed minimum f(0)=0. No prescribed plateau, ramp,
curvature pattern, knot count, or sign-crossing history is assumed.

For every m>=17, every x_0!=0, and the complete GD schedule H_m,

    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2 <= [1/(a c)]c^(-m).                (2)

The function g itself may depend on the entire horizon m. Consequently the
supremum of these objective ratios over the full class (1), divided by the
exact U_(N_m)^ZJ, tends to zero. This is a class obstruction, not a bound
on the unrestricted W_(N_m)(H_m).

## 2. A scalar clock and the quantitative closure inequality

The identically zero g has zero objective. Otherwise g(R)>0 for every R>0:
if it vanished at one positive radius, (1) and monotonicity would make it
vanish on arbitrarily large intervals. The continuous ratio g(R)/R is
periodic in log R. Thus its maximum M>0 is attained on any multiplicative
period, and M<=1 by smoothness. Put ell=1/M>=1.

Define a flow clock

    F(R)=integral_1^R dt/g(t),        R>0.

The arbitrary reference 1 affects only an additive constant. Equation (1)
implies that

    F(cR)-F(R)=tau>0                                        (3)

is independent of R: differentiate in R, or substitute t=cu. Choose r>=17
minimal with b=b_r>=ell. Then b/c<ell<=b. Define

    D=max_(R>0) [F(b g(R))-F(R)].                           (4)

The expression in brackets is periodic under R->cR, so the maximum exists
on a compact radial period. At a maximizer of g(R)/R=1/ell, one has
b g(R)>=R. Therefore D>=0.

The key proved inequality is

    tau+D <=(c-1)b.                                        (5)

Here is a direct proof for every possible input radius; no functional
inequality is left as an assumption. Fix R>0, put u=g(R)>0 and q=R/u>=1.

If q<=b, monotonicity gives

    F(bu)-F(R)<=b-q.                                       (6)

On the period [R,cR], the endpoint gradients are u and cu. Monotonicity and
the 1-Lipschitz condition give the pointwise lower bound

    g(t)>=max(u, cu-(cR-t)).

The switch occurs at t=cR-(c-1)u>=R. Integrating the reciprocal of this
lower bound yields

    tau<= (c-1)(q-1)+log c.                                (7)

Adding (6)-(7) gives

    tau+F(bu)-F(R)
       <=b+(c-2)q-(c-1)+log c
       <=(c-1)b-(c-1)+log c <(c-1)b,

because q<=b, c>2 and log c<c-1.

If q>=b, use (3) to write the same quantity as

    tau+F(bu)-F(R)=F(cbu)-F(R).

When cbu<R this quantity is negative. Otherwise every point of integration
has gradient at least u, so it is at most cb-q<=(c-1)b. This proves (5)
uniformly in R, and hence for the actual maximum D in (4).

## 3. Actual block and checkpoint updates of the same clock

Every step of H_r is at most b/c<ell. Since g(R)<=R/ell, these steps cannot
cross zero from a nonzero point. For a positive-radius step
R'=R-hg(R)>0, monotonicity gives

    F(R)-F(R')=integral_(R')^R dt/g(t)
        >=(R-R')/g(R)=h.                                  (8)

The same calculation applies after reflecting a negative point, because f
is even. Therefore each actual H_r block decreases the clock of the absolute
radius by at least S=s_r.

For a checkpoint of level k, h=b c^k, consider the actual next radius.
If it does not cross zero, its clock decreases. If it crosses, then

    R'=h g(R)-R <= c^k b g(R).

By (3)-(4), this gives

    F(R')-F(R)<=k tau+D.                                   (9)

Since D>=0, (9) also bounds a same-sign checkpoint. A zero landing has zero
terminal objective and can be excluded from the clock calculation.

For r>=17, one has S>=eta b with eta=13/8. To check this directly, note
(10/13)^3<1/2 and (10/13)^2<4/5, hence (10/13)^17<1/40; substitute in
s_r/b_r=[1-(2/c)^r]/(c-2). Since eta>c-1=8/5, (5) gives

    S-tau-D>0.                                            (10)

Thus the growing-state flow description has a genuine, uniformly profitable
update. It is not just a restatement of the unknown scalar optimization.

## 4. Sum the exact recursive word and retain the original normalization

First suppose m>=r and put J=m-r. The word H_m contains 2^J copies of H_r.
Its checkpoint levels k=0,...,J-1 occur 2^(J-k-1) times. Their total level
sum is 2^J-J-1, and their number is 2^J-1. Adding (8)-(9) along the full
actual trajectory gives

    F(|x_(N_m)|)-F(|x_0|)
       <=-2^J S+(2^J-J-1)tau+(2^J-1)D
       =-2^J(S-tau-D)-(J+1)tau-D
       <=-(J+1)tau.                                      (11)

Equation (3) and monotonicity of F imply

    |x_(N_m)|<=c^(-J-1)|x_0|.                             (12)

The global secant bound g(R)<=R/ell supplies the complete area bound
f(x)<=x^2/(2ell). Since ell>b/c,

    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2
       <=ell^(-1)c^(-2J-2)
       <=b^(-1)c^(-2J-1)
       =a^(-1)c^(-m-J-1)
       <=[1/(a c)]c^(-m).                                (13)

If m<r, every step in H_m is smaller than ell and the entire trajectory
stays on its initial ray. Let w=g(|x_(N_m)|) and R=|x_(N_m)|. Monotonicity
and smoothness give R+s_m w<=|x_0| and f(x_(N_m))<=Rw-w^2/2. Thus

    2f(x_(N_m))/|x_0|^2<=1/(1+2s_m)
       <=[1/(2eta a)]c^(-m)
       <=[1/(a c)]c^(-m),

using m>=17 and 2eta>c. This proves (2) in every case. All distances refer
to the same fixed minimizer zero. Scaling f by L gives exactly the project's
normalization and GD steps h_t/L.

## 5. Explicit exact-profile comparison and finite-knot realizations

For completeness set C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Their shifted
joins are Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2 and
G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. The bound
Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) reduces to
t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. With x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3),
the difference from 1 is x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0:
both reciprocal terms are at least 1/3 and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives
C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Since G(A,D)<=D+4A and the power 4/3 is superadditive on
nonnegative inputs, the dominant DP gives D_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Multiplying (2)
by D_(N_m) proves the claimed vanishing ratio, because c^3>16.

If the profile has finitely many affine pieces within one radial period,
any one finite nonzero trajectory has a finite-knot realization preserving
all its values and gradients. Choose a radius R below every nonzero query
and terminal radius, and put u=g(R)>0 and A=f(R). Smoothness and monotonicity
give

    u^2/2<=A<=Ru-u^2/2.

Replace the inner profile by zero up to
delta=R-u/2-A/u, then a unit-slope ramp of length u, then a plateau at u up
to R. The displayed bounds imply 0<=delta<=R-u. Its terminal gradient is u
and its area is Ru-delta u-u^2/2=A. All larger-radius gradients and values
are therefore unchanged. Apply the same replacement on both rays and cap
outside the largest sampled radius. The original finite GD trajectory and
its exact objective normalization are preserved. This does not claim that
an arbitrary nonhomogeneous core replacement obeys the same global theorem.

The new obstruction includes arbitrary legal within-period geometry,
horizon-dependent shape and scale, dense gradient values, arbitrarily many
knots, and every crossing pattern. It is still restricted to even functions
with the exact radial period c=13/5. The independently phased staircase
result in research/max3_multiscale_flow_obstruction.md remains a separate
asymmetric extension. Neither result decides the full scalar class or the
project's unrestricted all-schedule minimax problem.

## DOC-002624 — project/research/max3_geometric_relay_obstruction.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\max3_geometric_relay_obstruction.md

原始字节数：8591；原始 SHA-256：03c67fa359a55c1b9fcd8180dd43304d1df8cae3fe96b6a4232e2a40fd6597e6

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002624 -->
# Geometric gradient ladders cannot sustain the full recursive crossing pattern

Status: complete scoped candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No new verifier
call. This is a growing-complexity scalar obstruction with an explicit gradient
recurrence. It does not assume nested inward returns. Its hypotheses are the
specified crossing pattern and a geometric grid for gradients on one ray.

## 1. The schedule and the class being tested

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_u=a c^u, H_0=(), and

    H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    s_u=sum H_u=a(c^u-2^u)/(c-2).

Fix m>r>=18, set J=m-r, b=b_r, and S=s_r. The exact word H_m consists
of 2^J copies of H_r separated by 2^J-1 checkpoint steps. Their levels form

    T_0=(),       T_(j+1)=(T_j,j,T_j),

where a level ell stands for the actual step b c^ell. In zero-based order,
every even-indexed checkpoint has level zero. The odd-indexed checkpoints
have levels ell>=1; level ell occurs 2^(J-ell-1) times for 1<=ell<=J-1.
The last checkpoint has level zero.

Let f be ANY fixed differentiable convex scalar function with 1-Lipschitz
derivative, f(0)=0=min f. Assume its GD trajectory starts at x_0>0 and has
positive terminal objective. Impose the following two trajectory hypotheses:

1. Every displayed checkpoint crosses zero strictly; no step within a copy
   of H_r crosses zero. Gradients inside those copies may vary arbitrarily.
2. The positive gradient magnitudes just before the level-zero checkpoints
   all belong to one grid {theta gamma^z : z is an integer}, with theta>0
   and gamma>=c. Both parameters may depend on the complete horizon.

There is no grid assumption on negative gradients, no symmetry assumption on
f, no fixed core curvature, and no restriction on whether a return is inward,
overlapping, or outward. The number of crossings is 2^J-1 and can grow with m.

Then the normalized terminal objective satisfies

    2f(x_(2^m-1))/x_0^2
      <= [(b-1)^2/((S+1)^2(2S+1))] gamma^(2-2J)
      <= [1/(2 eta^3 b)] gamma^(2-2J),   eta=13/8.            (1)

In particular, uniformly over r, all gradient-grid parameters, every legal
choice of knots and all such trajectories,

    2f(x_(2^m-1))/x_0^2 <= [c/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m).           (2)

The ratio of the supremum in (2) to the exact U_(2^m-1)^ZJ tends to zero.

## 2. Proof of the exact gradient recurrence

For a same-sign block of total step sum S ending at gradient magnitude v,
all earlier magnitudes in that block are at least v and its final radius is
at least v. Consequently its starting radius A satisfies

    A >= (S+1)v.                                           (3)

A crossing step beta whose input gradient magnitude is u has landing radius
at most (beta-1)u, since the input radius is at least u. Thus two consecutive
checkpoints of levels (0,ell), and the two intervening same-sign H_r blocks,
give the following bound between their successive positive checkpoint
gradient magnitudes u and u_next:

    u_next/u <= [(b-1)(b c^ell-1)]/(S+1)^2.                 (4)

This follows by applying (3) first on the negative block and then on the
positive block. It does not make any assumption about their derivative
values between endpoints beyond monotonicity on each ray.

Since (2/c)^17<1/40, we have S>=eta b for r>=18. Also

    eta^2=169/64 > 13/5=c.

Therefore (4) is strictly smaller than

    c^ell/eta^2 < c^(ell-1) <= gamma^(ell-1).                (5)

Hypothesis 2 makes u_next/u an integer power of gamma. It follows that

    u_next/u <= gamma^(ell-2).                             (6)

This strict-integer step is the essential use of the geometric grid. Without
the grid, (5) alone does not imply (6).

Pair the checkpoints as (0,ell), leaving the final level-zero checkpoint
unpaired. The total exponent in (6) is

    sum_(ell=1)^(J-1) (ell-2)2^(J-ell-1)=1-J.               (7)

For example this sum is empty and zero for J=1. In general it follows by
subtracting twice sum 2^(J-ell-1)=2^(J-1)-1 from
sum ell 2^(J-ell-1)=2^J-J-1. Thus if u_first and u_last denote the positive
gradient magnitudes at the first and last checkpoints,

    u_last <= gamma^(1-J) u_first.                         (8)

The first H_r gives x_0>=(S+1)u_first. After the last checkpoint the negative
landing radius A is at most (b-1)u_last. The remaining H_r stays on that ray.
Let w be its terminal gradient magnitude and R its terminal radius. Then
R+S w<=A. Integrating the globally fixed derivative on this ray gives

    f(x_n)<=Rw-w^2/2,
    2f(x_n)<=2A w-(2S+1)w^2<=A^2/(2S+1).                  (9)

Indeed the derivative magnitude at radius t<=R is at most min(t,w). This
includes all terminal area and keeps the minimizer fixed at zero. Combining
(8)-(9) proves the first bound in (1). The second follows from S>=eta b.

Since J>=1 and gamma>=c, gamma^(2-2J)<=c^(2-2J). Substituting b=a c^r
and m=r+J bounds (1) by

    [1/(2 eta^3 a)] c^(-m) c^(2-J)
       <=[c/(2 eta^3 a)]c^(-m),

which proves (2). The proof applies even when r and all profile parameters
change with m. No finite-parameter compactness claim is being substituted.

## 3. Why a homothetic staircase relay necessarily enters this obstruction

The proposed self-similar positive-ray profile uses plateau heights

    M_z=theta gamma^z

at radial intervals [ell_0 M_z,u_0 M_z], where 1<=ell_0<=u_0. Adjacent
plateaus can be connected by a nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz derivative only if

    gamma ell_0-u_0 >= gamma-1.                            (10)

Equality corresponds to a slope-one connecting ramp; strict inequality
allows gentler or nonconstant connecting slopes. Together with an arbitrary
legal negative-ray derivative this defines a globally fixed convex smooth
function. The infinite staircase extends continuously to zero; alternatively
one can retain the finitely many relevant levels and attach a legal inner
core and outer cap before the run. All endpoints and slopes must be fixed.

Consider the proposed relay in which each positive-ray H_r block remains on
one of these plateaus, and every displayed checkpoint crosses. Its motion
during such a block has radial length S M_z. Therefore the plateau width
must satisfy

    u_0-ell_0 >= S.

Together with (10), this forces

    (gamma-1)(ell_0-1)>=S.                                 (11)

At the first, level-zero checkpoint, the departure radius is at least
ell_0 M_z. A strict crossing with step b requires ell_0<b. Hence

    gamma > 1+S/(b-1) > 1+eta =21/8 > c.                   (12)

The same positive profile therefore satisfies the grid hypothesis of
Section 1 automatically. Its negative ray and every negative H_r block may
be arbitrary subject to the stated sign pattern. The construction may use
an increasing number of plateaus, may repeatedly revisit them, may have
different curvatures between them, and may change all its parameters with
the horizon. It is nevertheless bounded by (1)-(2).

This is a concrete failure of the intended multiscale plateau relay: the
geometric spacing needed to accommodate an entire ordinary block forces a
full gradient-level loss at the frequent low checkpoints. The rarer high
checkpoints cannot compensate; their exact total exponent is 1-J.

## 4. Exact-profile comparison and boundary of the result

Here is a self-contained sufficient benchmark estimate. For the exact DP set
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Their joins are

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

The inequality Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) reduces to
t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1. With x=t^(1/3) and
y=(1-t)^(1/3), its difference from 1 is
x^3 y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0: each reciprocal is at least 1/3
and xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3. Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Since
G(A,D)<=D+4A and u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3), induction in the dominant
DP gives D_n<=1+4n^(4/3). Now c^3>16, so multiplying (2) by D_(2^m-1)
proves that the normalized exact-profile ratio tends to zero.

The theorem does NOT cover arbitrary geometric-gradient profiles when their
trajectory skips one of the prescribed crossings, crosses inside H_r, or
has positive checkpoint gradients outside the stated grid. It does NOT
exclude general scalar functions, and it does NOT bound multidimensional W.
In particular the already shared coupled-coordinate separation is compatible
with it.

A next scalar construction must evade the explicit lost-grid-level step:
for example by traversing several knots inside the positive H_r blocks with
dense gradient levels, or by altering the coarse crossing pattern. Merely
adding more homothetic plateau levels does not evade the proof.

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002625 -->
# A quantitative obstruction to chronological-window gluing

Status: complete explicit candidate proof; not yet verifier-accepted.
Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker: max3.

## Exact scope

An exact conditional completion state for smooth-convex GD histories cannot
consist only of the initial jet, minimizer, prescribed schedule, every past point
and gradient, and any fixed-length chronological window of function values.
There are two legal histories with identical such retained data but conditional
terminal objective maxima differing by a factor of 16. The missing information
in this example is exactly one old function value.

This does not exclude other finite-dimensional state representations, including
states that encode the missing interpolation constraint. It does not determine
the unrestricted worst case of the displayed schedule, nor the minimax target.

## Explicit functions and legal histories

Normalize L=1, x_star=0, and f_star=0. Define

\[
F(x)=\begin{cases}
x^2/2,&x\leq0,\\
0,&0\leq x\leq1,\\
(x-1)^2/2,&1\leq x\leq3,\\
2x-4,&x\geq3.
\end{cases}
\]

Let G(x)=integral from 0 to x of q(t) dt, where

\[
q(t)=\begin{cases}
t,&t\leq1/2,\\
1/2,&1/2\leq t\leq11/6,\\
t-4/3,&11/6\leq t\leq10/3,\\
2,&t\geq10/3.
\end{cases}
\]

The derivatives of F and G are continuous and nondecreasing and have slopes
only 0 or 1. They are globally 1-Lipschitz. Both functions are differentiable,
convex, and have a minimizer at 0 with value 0. The functions coincide on the
negative half-line.

At 4, both functions have value 4 and derivative 2. For G, direct integration
gives G(11/6)=19/24, G(10/3)=19/24+15/8=8/3, and G(4)=4.
At p=3/2 their common derivative is 1/2, but

\[
F(p)=1/8,\qquad G(p)=5/8.
\]

Fix an integer m>=1. With x_0=4 use the predetermined schedule

\[
h_0=5/4,\quad h_1=5,\quad
h_{2+j}=1/2\quad(0\leq j<m),\quad
h_{m+2}=1+(5/4)2^m.
\]

All m+3 steps are strictly positive and finite. Under either function the
prefix iterates and derivatives coincide:

\[
x_1=4-(5/4)2=3/2,
\quad x_2=3/2-5(1/2)=-1,
\quad x_{2+j}=-2^{-j}\quad(0\leq j\leq m).
\]

For the negative tail, the derivative equals the coordinate, so each step
1/2 halves the coordinate. At the cut k=m+2 the common point and derivative
are -2^{-m}. The last step takes both trajectories to

\[
y=-2^{-m}+[1+(5/4)2^m]2^{-m}=5/4.
\]

All sampled prefix function values coincide except the value at x_1=p.
For any prescribed finite chronological window choose m long enough that p
lies outside it. This gives identical retained data even if every sampled
point and gradient, the initial jet, minimizer, radius, and full schedule are
also retained. No repeated points or zero steps are needed.

## Exact conditional terminal maxima

A completion of a prefix means any differentiable convex function on R with
1-Lipschitz derivative that interpolates every point, value and derivative in
that prefix and has f(0)=0 and f'(0)=0. Its final query point is y=5/4 because
the cut data and last step are fixed.

For an F-prefix completion f, smoothness gives

\[
f'(t)\geq f'(p)-(p-t)=t-1\qquad(y\leq t\leq p).
\]

Integrating gives

\[
f(y)=f(p)-\int_y^p f'(t)\,dt
\leq\frac18-\int_{5/4}^{3/2}(t-1)\,dt
=\frac18-\frac3{32}=\frac1{32}.
\]

F attains this bound, hence the conditional maximum is 1/32.

For a G-prefix completion f, smoothness from 0 and monotonicity up to p give

\[
f'(t)\leq\min\{t,1/2\}\qquad(0\leq t\leq p).
\]

The integral of the right side equals 1/8+1/2=5/8, exactly f(p)-f(0).
The continuous nonnegative difference between this upper envelope and f'
therefore has integral zero and is identically zero. Consequently

\[
f(y)=\int_0^{5/4}\min\{t,1/2\}\,dt
=\frac18+\frac12\left(\frac54-\frac12\right)=\frac12.
\]

G is a completion, so the second conditional maximum is exactly 1/2.
Their ratio is 16. Since |x_0-x_star|^2=16, the normalized conditional
objective values 2(f(y)-f_star)/|x_0-x_star|^2 are 1/256 and 1/16.

## Consequence for a split rule

Take the full F prefix and the G continuation. The continuation is legal and
agrees with every retained datum listed above, but no single legal function can
realize their union: the first conditional bound forbids the continuation's
value 1/2 at y. Thus matching those retained data is not a lossless gluing
criterion. Any proposed exact state compression must retain the old value's
constraint, or prove an equivalent replacement. This construction does not
preclude a different compressed state or an argument at the unconditional
maximized value level.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002626 -->
# Two-cut inner returns and an exact rejection of the paired four-copy seed

Status: complete explicit candidate proof, not verifier-accepted. No additional
verification allocation is assumed. This refutes a proposed primitive schedule;
it is not a negative or positive resolution of the unrestricted minimax target.

## 1. Exact legal inner-return construction

Partition any nonnegative schedule chronologically into

    (block_0, b_0, block_1, b_1, block_2),

with ordinary block sums s_0,s_1,s_2. Choose numbers 0<w<=1 and R>=w.
Put

    r_outer=R+1-w,
    A=b_0-r_outer=b_0-1-R+w,
    v=A/(1+s_1),
    z=(b_1-1)v.

Require A>0 and

    (1+s_2)w <= z <= R.                                    (1)

The following SINGLE derivative is fixed before running the schedule:

    g(x)=-v                     for x<=-v,
    g(x)=x                      for -v<=x<=w,
    g(x)=w                      for w<=x<=R,
    g(x)=x-R+w                  for R<=x<=r_outer,
    g(x)=1                      for x>=r_outer.             (2)

It is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz: the successive slopes are
0,1,0,1,0 and endpoint values agree. Define f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.
Then f is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth, and minimized at zero with f(0)=0.
Explicitly,

    f(x)=-v x-v^2/2                            for x<=-v,
    f(x)=x^2/2                                 for -v<=x<=w,
    f(x)=w x-w^2/2                             for w<=x<=R,
    f(x)=(x-R+w)^2/2+wR-w^2                    for R<=x<=r_outer,
    f(x)=x-r_outer+1/2+wR-w^2                   for x>=r_outer.

Start at x_0=s_0+r_outer. The entire first block has gradient1 and reaches
r_outer. The first checkpoint reaches -A. The middle block has gradient -v:
every partial sum is at most s_1, so it stays in [-A,-v] and ends at -v.
The second checkpoint reaches z. By (1), the final block stays on the inner
positive plateau [w,R], with gradient w, and ends at

    x_n=z-s_2 w >= w.

All individual steps, including zero steps, obey the supplied GD recursion.
The exact normalized terminal objective is

    2 f(x_n)/x_0^2
       = [2wz-(2s_2+1)w^2]/(s_0+R+1-w)^2.                 (3)

This differs from the verified outward-ray C6 construction: the second return
can have gradient w strictly smaller than the old positive gradient1. The old
positive plateau lies farther out. The connecting ramp and its area cannot be
discarded in a primitive-energy calculation.

## 2. Exact primitive energy, retaining the remote area

Let s=s_0+b_0+s_1+b_1+s_2. The saturated intrinsic primitive energy is

    V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
          +sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)g_i^2-s(s+1)g_n^2.          (4)

A primitive schedule must make (4) nonnegative for every legal smooth convex
trajectory. Set Q_i=f_i-x_i g_i+g_i^2/2. Expanding
x_(i+1)^2=x_i^2-2h_i x_i g_i+h_i^2g_i^2 and summing gives the exact identity

    V_h=x_0^2-x_n^2-2s f_n-s(s+1)g_n^2+2sum_(i<n)h_i Q_i.

For all first-block samples and the first checkpoint query, (2) gives

    Q_i=-(1-w)(R-w).

For every remaining sample, including the terminal sample, Q_i=0, since its
ray is a constant extension of the unit quadratic core. Also g_n=w and
f_n=w x_n-w^2/2. Therefore

    V_h=(s_0+R+1-w)^2
          -[z+(s_0+b_0+s_1+b_1)w]^2
          -2(s_0+b_0)(1-w)(R-w).                          (5)

The last term is the contribution of the old positive area. It is negative
and is required by the exact function values. Formula (5), with constraints
(1), is a new necessary scalar test for any claimed primitive schedule.
It is not a sufficient certificate.

## 3. The actual eleven-step candidate is not primitive

The candidate in finding0bdb4dfb180b15a0 is

    h=(3/2,3/2,19/5,3/2,3/2,5,3/2,3/2,9/5,3/2,3/2).

Its sum is s=113/5 and shifted sum is118/5. Choose the two checkpoints19/5
and5, so s_0=s_1=3 and s_2=39/5. Set

    w=1/6,       R=89/60,       r_outer=139/60,
    v=89/240,    z=R,          x_0=319/60.

Here A=89/60=4v and (1+s_2)w=22/15=88/60<R, so all feasibility conditions
hold. The complete exact GD data are:

| i | x_i | g_i | f_i |
|---|---|---|---|
|0|319/60|1|1339/360|
|1|229/60|1|799/360|
|2|139/60|1|259/360|
|3|-89/60|-89/240|55447/115200|
|4|-89/96|-89/240|7921/28800|
|5|-89/240|-89/240|7921/115200|
|6|89/60|1/6|7/30|
|7|37/30|1/6|23/120|
|8|59/60|1/6|3/20|
|9|41/60|1/6|1/10|
|10|13/30|1/6|7/120|
|11|11/60|1/6|1/60|

Every row follows from (2) and x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. In particular the first
two ordinary pairs use the outer positive and negative plateaus, while the
last five steps use the lower positive plateau. Direct substitution in (4),
or the shorter identity (5), gives

    V_h=(319/60)^2-(79/20)^2
          -2(34/5)(5/6)(79/60)
        = -508/225 < 0.                                    (6)

The literal primitive property of this eleven-step schedule is false. The
counterexample is to the actual paired seed, not merely to the generic join
rule with an empty seed. The shifted-sum advantage therefore cannot be used
in the conditional primitive-seed amplification argument.

The objective error of this particular instance is only
2f_11/x_0^2=120/319^2. Thus (6) must not be presented as a strong lower bound on
its worst-case objective or as a disproof of some different direct objective
certificate. It specifically blocks using this schedule as a primitive seed.

## 4. A compact symbolic slice explaining the failure

Keep s_0=s_1=3, b_0=19/5, b_1=5, s_2=39/5 and impose z=R. Then

    R=7/5+w/2,       0<w<=14/83.

Formula (5) becomes

    V_h=204/25-(112/5)w-(6016/25)w^2.

The rational choice w=1/6 lies in this interval and gives (6). This is an
analytic family, not a numerical sign inferred near a floating-point zero.
The finite rational calculation of all twelve rows was independently run as
an arithmetic sanity check; no SDP, sweep, or verifier call was used.

## 5. The same four-copy rule fails for every seed, not only the rational pair

Let a_seed be ANY finite nonnegative schedule with sum A-1, where A>=1.
Form

    J(a_seed)=[a_seed,1+(7/10)A,a_seed,1+A,
               a_seed,1+(1/5)A,a_seed].

Use the first two displayed bridges as the checkpoints in Section1; the last
bridge is part of the final constant-gradient block. Thus

    s_0=s_1=A-1,  s_2=(11/5)A-1,
    b_0=1+(7/10)A,  b_1=1+A.

Choose

    w=7/44,       R=7A/20+7/88,       v=R/A,       z=R.

We have R>=w, A_landing=b_0-1-R+w=R, and
(1+s_2)w=7A/20<R. These are exactly the hypotheses of Section1, regardless
of the internal steps of a_seed. Each copy used before or between checkpoints
stays on its assigned plateau because its partial sums are nonnegative and
at most its total. The last two copies and intervening bridge remain on the
lower positive plateau by the same argument.

For general bridge factors c and e with middle bridge1+A, this z=R slice has

    R=(cA+w)/2,
    V_J/A^2=1-c^2-2cw-[(3+c)^2+2(2+c)/A]w^2.              (7)

Substituting c=7/10 and the displayed w into (7) gives the exact expression

    V_J=-(2293/38720)A^2-(1323/9680)A < 0.                 (8)

Hence this particular four-copy rule cannot be repaired by choosing a
different primitive seed, a larger seed, or a seed with a different quadratic
product. No primitive property of the input seed was used in the refutation.
The obstruction concerns the exact rule with bridge factors7/10,1,1/5; it
does not cover all possible four-copy rules.
