<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002645 -->
# Exact retained storage from a two-step primitive block

Status: unverified research identities. No new verifier or numerical PEP call has been used. These formulas do not yet certify an asymptotic improvement.

All functions below are convex and differentiable with 1-Lipschitz gradient. Write

    Q_ij = f_i-f_j + <g_i,x_j-x_i> + ||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0.

## 1. Primitive dual residual, with the actual interpolation coefficients

For a GD block of nonnegative physical steps h, let S=sum h_i, A=S+1, d=x_0-x_n, p=g_0 and q=g_n. Define its saturated primitive energy

    V = 2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)
        + sum_(i<n) h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2 - S A ||q||^2.

The identity below is algebraic for every block; the primitive assumption is V>=0. Put

    H = 2(f_0-f_n)-2S||q||^2-||p-(d+q)/A||^2,
    A_start = -2[sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,0)+Q_(n,0)],
    A_end   = -2 sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,n).

Then

    H = (2A-1)/A^2 V + A_start/A + (A-1)/A^2 A_end.       (1)

In particular H>=0 for an actual primitive block. This follows without importing a quadratic-product condition or identifying the ambient primitive and s-composable classes.

For a direct check, put Z=d+q and use proof weights (h,1). Their intrinsic energy is V+A(A-1)||q||^2. Expanding the two interpolation sums gives

    A_start = 2A(f_0-f_n)-V-A(A-1)||q||^2
              -||Z||^2+2<Z,p>-A||p||^2,
    A_end = -V+||Z||^2-A^2||q||^2.

Substituting proves (1). This is the exact identity obtained before the optimum interpolation is added in Grimmer--Shu--Wang, arXiv:2410.16249, Appendix A.1, Lemma17. That proof was read, including its definitions and Proposition6 input; the calculation here checks applicability to the present V definition.

## 2. The pair (3/2,3/2) has a sharp nonzero boundary remainder

For this specific two-step block let p=g_0, m=g_1, q=g_2. Its displacement is d=(3/2)(p+m), and S=3, A=4. Define the retained displacement error

    t=d-3q=(3/2)(p+m-2q).

The exact certificate is

    V = 4(-Q_10)+9(-Q_12)+16(-Q_21)+(-Q_02)
        + ||t||^2/9.                                (2)

All coefficients are nonnegative. Expanding each Q verifies the function coefficients (3,3,-6), gradient diagonal coefficients (-3/4,-3/4,-12), and zero off-diagonal coefficients after adding the displayed square. Thus

    V >= ||d-3q||^2/9.                               (3)

The coefficient 1/9 is sharp over all legal functions. Take the one-dimensional function with derivative g(x)=x for x<=1 and g(x)=1 for x>=1, normalized by f(0)=0. It is convex, 1-smooth, and minimized at zero. Starting at x_0=5/2 gives x_1=1, x_2=-1/2, gradients (1,1,-1/2), and values (2,1/2,1/8). Therefore V=9/4 and t=9/2, giving V=||t||^2/9.

This is a new retained term, not merely a second naming of the objective residual. It vanishes on both the flat-gradient and full-curvature equality trajectories, but need not vanish on a general trajectory.

## 3. Objective and dual slacks are correlated

For the same pair, set r=p-(d+q)/4 and H=2(f_0-f_2)-6||q||^2-||r||^2. Equation (1) becomes

    H = (7/16)V + (3/4)(-Q_10)+(1/2)(-Q_20)
                    +(9/16)(-Q_02)+(9/16)(-Q_12).   (4)

There is also the exact reverse-side combination

    V-(4/5)H
      = (2/5)(-Q_01)+(9/5)(-Q_10)+(26/5)(-Q_12)
          +(48/5)(-Q_21)+||t||^2/36.                (5)

Thus this actual block supplies both H>=0 and

    V >= (4/5)H + ||t||^2/36.                        (6)

Unlike separate assumed squared penalties, (5) preserves a concrete correlation between the objective energy and the dual slack. Every actual interpolation multiplier is displayed; no negative interpolation inequality is used.

Equivalently, with Delta=f_0-f_2 and boundary vector (p,d,q),

    V-(8/5)Delta >= (p,d,q)^T J (p,d,q),

    J = [ -4/5    1/5     1/5  ]
        [  1/5   -1/45   -2/15 ]
        [  1/5   -2/15   -23/5 ].                   (7)

Here the matrix is an inherited storage term and is not asserted PSD. Discarding it or resetting its displacement coordinate at a later join would be invalid. The same clipped-quadratic example in part2 attains (5) at equality: H=135/64, V=9/4 and t^2/36=9/16.

For checking (2) and (5), the following one-parameter coefficient identity is useful. For any real kappa,

    V = (1-4kappa)(-Q_01)+(1/2+2kappa)(-Q_02)
        +(7/2+2kappa)(-Q_10)+(17/2+2kappa)(-Q_12)
        +(1-4kappa)(-Q_20)+(14+8kappa)(-Q_21)
        + kappa ||p+m-2q||^2.                       (8)

Equation (2) uses kappa=1/4. Combining (8) with (4), using kappa=5/52 in the factor (13/20)V, proves (5). This was checked by a fixed nine-equation rational coefficient calculation, not by an optimization or numerical search.

## 4. Calibration also recovers the full dominant recursion

The existing exact join in max4_two_sided_residual_join.md recovers ConPP. Its ordinary ConPD specialization does not require an independent tail residual penalty.

Let the prefix be genuinely primitive with capacity K, energy V_a and weights (a,K), so D_a=2K-1 and e_a=K g. Let the tail have arbitrary nonnegative intrinsic proof weights v, mass D_b>0 and energy E_b>=0. Define

    beta=(1+sqrt(1+8K/D_b))/2,
    c=2K/D_b=beta^2-beta,
    tau=1+K/beta.

Keep the prefix physical weights, change its terminal weight from K to K+1, and scale every tail weight by beta. Its new mass is

    D_new=2K+beta D_b=beta^2 D_b=G(K,D_b).

For A_y=-2 sum_(i in tail) v_i Q_(i,y), where y is the bridge start, direct expansion gives

    E_new=V_a+beta^2 E_b+c A_y.                       (9)

Indeed the general join has eta=1, gamma=0, m=t_y=t_z=0; its quadratic remainder has coefficients -2K+cD_b=0 on g^2, beta(1-beta)+c=0 on U_b^2, and beta(tau-1-K)+c(tau-1)=0 on <g,U_b>. All actual weights and interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. The standard squared-distance identity therefore gives W<=1/D_new.

Consequently the exact boundary interface recovers both scalar F and G operations and hence every finite-horizon DP denominator B_n, not merely a dyadic exponent or a rate with a fixed asymptotic loss. Equations (2)--(7) are extra block information; their profitable propagation is the outstanding construction task.

## 5. Terminal-storage literature: a precise finite reduction

Scherer, arXiv:2207.01363, Theorem5.1 in the HTML numbering (Theorem11 in the main-agent PDF pointer), equations30--35 and AppendixA.5, reduces its finite terminal-cost expression to a doubly hyperdominant matrix M: off-diagonal entries are nonpositive and all row and column sums are nonnegative. Its filter is initialized at zero. The proof and the conjecture in Section7 were read; no completeness conjecture or nonzero-initial-state extension is used here.

The connection to the project's actual interpolation cone can be checked directly. For samples including a minimizer, translate x_star=0 and f_star=0 and set

    z_i=x_i-g_i, F_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

Then Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,z_j-z_i>. For a doubly hyperdominant M on the non-optimum samples define weights

    a_ij=-M_ij (i!=j),
    a_i,star=sum_j M_ij,
    a_star,i=sum_j M_ji.

All weights are nonnegative. Their incoming and outgoing masses agree at every vertex, including the optimum. Thus the F coefficients cancel, and exactly

    sum_(i,j) a_ij(-Q_ij)=sum_(i,j) M_ij<g_i,z_j>.   (10)

The finite hyperdominant terminal form therefore gives an actual combination of the existing smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. It is a potentially useful parametrization of retained correlations, not an additional nonlinear premise. Equation (10) says nothing about completeness of the five-variable join or of any finite memory compression.

At a split, a one-step memory example already shows what must be retained:

    sum_(i=m)^n <g_i,z_i-z_(i-1)> -(F_n-F_(m-1))
      =sum_(i=m)^n (-Q_(i,i-1)) >=0.                (11)

Both z_(m-1) and F_(m-1) are inherited. Replacing them by zero at an internal join changes the identity. For this project, the concrete new candidate storage is (7), with the displacement and both endpoint gradients retained, rather than a bare appeal to a stability theorem.

## Current boundary

There is still no closed improving family. The next direct attempt is to transport the actual pair storage through more than one bridge while permitting an indefinite intermediate storage matrix, and to require a valid nonnegative final certificate in the original objective normalization. All intermediate identities in this note remain unverified; they must be supplied in full in any eventual verification package.

# A new full-cone endpoint prism excluded by the original repaired cut

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED, max4 round27. The witness
mechanism is the previously published C83 repaired cut, not a new witness
formula. It is rederived completely below. The new result is the following
capacity-uniform exclusion prism, prompted by charged discovery case31.
No numerical output is a premise of the proof, and no fact_submit is authorized.

Write h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=1+sum h=5+a+b+c+d. There is NO full primitive
certificate from accepted fact04219554e41d9750 when

    C>=193/25,
    27/50<=a<=14/25,
    69/100<=b<=71/100,
    c>0, d>0.                                             (1)

There is no upper bound on C, no relation or ordering between c and d,
and no restriction on any of the twenty nonnegative multipliers or on the
rank of the full original five-gradient matrix. This is a restricted
step prism, not global four-step seed nonexistence.

## 1. Full interface, endpoint bound, and quadratic test

Let P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, s=P4, p_i=1+P_i, and let e be the five-entry
all-ones column. With the original twenty nonnegative Lambda_ij,

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                  (2)

All matrices act on the five independent formal gradients. This is the
accepted interface, which makes the original primitive energy

    V=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                               -s(s+1)||g4||^2

nonnegative on every actual convex differentiable1-smooth GD trajectory.

The identities Te=p and sum_i h_i P_i=(s^2-sum_i h_i^2)/2 give e^TMe=0.
Full PSD then forces Me=0 by expanding(e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs
of t. In particular, its terminal row moment is

    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                       (3)

The terminal diagonal in(2) is sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j-s(s+2)>=0.
Since s-P_j>=h3=1+d and s>0, (3) implies

    1+d<=2C/(C+1), hence d<1.                           (4)

Use the actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2, started at x0=1. Its gradients
are pquad_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t), and Tpquad=e. Therefore

    pquad^T M pquad=pquad^T D pquad
         =1-C^2 pquad_4^2.

The last equality telescopes
h_i(2-h_i)pquad_i^2=pquad_i^2-pquad_(i+1)^2.
All four excesses in(1) are positive, so full PSD requires

    C a b c d<=1.                                       (5)

These arguments use no compact-domain draft or prefix primitivity.

## 2. A scalar cut and the repaired two-dimensional cut

Put Q=3+a+b and eta=c/(2+d)>0. The scalar function with derivative
clamp(x,[-eta,1]), normalized to minimum0, has the actual trajectory

    xB=(Q,2+b,1,-c,-eta),
    gB=(1,1,1,-eta,-eta).

At every sample its value is fB_i=xB_i*gB_i-gB_i^2/2.
The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, so the
function is convex, differentiable and1-smooth. The four original GD
updates follow from c=(2+d)eta. Telescoping the primitive energy gives

    V_B=Q^2-C^2 eta^2.

Consequently every full certificate also obeys

    C eta<=Q.                                          (6)

On(1), b<1,Q<5,C>7, so(6) implies b eta<1.
We now rederive the actual all-pair repaired cut on this matched domain.
Set

    epsilon=(1+eta)/(a^2 b(1-b eta))>0,
    xA=(1,-a,ab,-abc,-ab eta),
    gA=(1,-a,ab,-ab eta,-ab eta),
    fA_i=xA_i*gA_i-gA_i^2/2.

Both scalar arrays follow the SAME actual four steps. Define actual
two-dimensional data

    X_i=(xB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*xA_i),
    G_i=(gB_i,sqrt(epsilon)*gA_i),
    F_i=fB_i+epsilon*fA_i,

and include the optimum X_star=G_star=0,F_star=0.

Here is a complete check of all original interpolation pairs, including
all optimum and terminal pairs. For saturated values f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2,
the original residual is Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j).
In B, targets0 and1 have nonnegative normal and maximal gradient1;
target3 has negative normal-(c-eta) and minimal gradient-eta;
targets2,4 and star have zero normal. Every B residual is nonpositive.
In A, only target3 has a nonzero normal, namely-ab(c-eta)<0.
Among all A gradients, only gA_1=-a lies below-ab eta, since b eta<1;
the others are1,ab,-ab eta,-ab eta,0. Thus the only positive A residual is

    Q_A(1,3)=a^2 b(1-b eta)(c-eta).

The same B pair has residual-(1+eta)(c-eta). The stated epsilon cancels
this pair exactly, and every other total residual is nonpositive.

For a direct realization, let K be the convex hull of the six vectors
G_star,G0,...,G4. Define

    F(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).

The pair inequalities just proved say that X_j-G_j is an outward normal
to K at G_j, for every j. Completing the square shows that the maximizer
is the Euclidean projection of X onto K; it is unique. Hence F(X_j)=F_j
and grad F(X_j)=G_j. Projection is1-Lipschitz: adding the two projection
normal inequalities gives ||G-H||^2<=<G-H,X-Y>. Thus this F is a convex
differentiable1-smooth function. Since0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0.
This constructs one actual function and all the GD updates, rather than
combining transcripts from different functions.

Both terminal positions equal their gradients. Telescoping the saturated
values in the original primitive energy therefore gives

    V=Q^2-C^2 eta^2+epsilon(1-C^2 a^2 b^2 eta^2)
      =(1+b)/(1-b eta) * (J-C^2 eta^2),
    J=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).              (7)

The coefficient identity uses (1-b eta)+b(1+eta)=1+b.
Full-certificate feasibility consequently requires C^2 eta^2<=J.
This is the old repaired-cut mechanism with all hypotheses and all pairs
reproduced; the new prism exclusion follows next.

## 3. Uniform symbolic contradiction on the whole prism

Put ksum=c+d=C-5-a-b. The bounds(1) imply

    ksum>=193/25-5-14/25-71/100=29/20,
    a b>=1863/5000,
    C a b>=359559/125000.

By(5),

    c d<=125000/359559<7/20,                            (8)

where 2500000<2516913 checks the last rational comparison.
If d>=31/100, then(4) places d in[31/100,1]. Concavity of
d(29/20-d) gives its minimum at an endpoint of this interval. Thus

    cd=d(ksum-d)>=min(1767/5000,9/20)>7/20,

contradicting(8). Hence

    d<31/100, c>57/50,
    eta=c/(2+d)>38/77.                                 (9)

Since C>=193/25>77/10, (9) yields

    C^2 eta^2>(19/5)^2=361/25.                          (10)

On the other hand Q<=427/100 gives Q^2<19, while

    a^2 b >=50301/250000>1/5,
    1/(a^2 b)<5.

Also Q>4 and b>=69/100 give bQ^2>5. Therefore the coefficient of eta
in J from(7), namely[1/(a^2 b)-bQ^2]/(1+b), is strictly negative.
Since eta>0,

    J<J(0)=(Q^2+1/(a^2 b))/(1+b)
       <24/(169/100)=2400/169<361/25.                   (11)

The last comparison is60000<61009. Equations(10)--(11) make the
actual legal energy(7) strictly negative. This contradicts the full
certificate, and proves the whole prism exclusion(1).

The proof retains ALL twenty multiplier signs and the full original
five-gradient PSD condition. It does not assume bc<1, impose a second
kernel, fix the capacity to193/25, or rely on numerical residual tolerance.

## 4. Discovery provenance and honest scope

Charged case31 selected the midpoint of a core endpoint component with
C=193/25,a=11/20,b=7/10. Its original C83 scalar test was negative, so
no conic solve was performed for that case. The entire rectangle in(1)
is now excluded by the textual inequalities above, not by extending the
sampled numerical sign. The initial step interval is[77/50,39/25], so
this prism lies outside the older h0 in[7/5,3/2] endpoint square; it is
also disjoint from the old projected prism a in[39/100,41/100],
b in[79/100,81/100]. These comparisons concern the stated step regions,
not a claim that all previous scalar obstructions were weaker.

This does not decide the remaining full-cone region. No full threshold
certificate or original minimax answer is present. All mathematical
assertions in this document remain a joint UNVERIFIED draft until a
separately authorized gate is selected. Accepted04219554e41d9750 is the
only mathematical predecessor used; no external result is imported.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002647 -->
# Whole-tail residual transport: exact rule and balanced-doubling failure

Status: self-contained unverified certificate algebra, a finite valid test, and a quantitative limitation of one explicitly specified recursion. The conclusion concerns the bound produced by this certificate rule, not the true worst case of the resulting schedules. No new numerical evaluation or verifier call.

Work at L=1. For any block h with nonnegative proof weights u on its iterates define D=sum u_i, d=x_start-x_end, U=sum u_i g_i, e=U-d, and

  E_h(u)=2sum_i u_i(f_i-f_end)+sum_i u_i||g_i||^2
         +2sum_i u_i<g_i,x_start-x_i>-||U||^2.

For every minimizer x_star, expansion gives

  ||x_start-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D(f_end-f_star)
     =||x_end-x_star-e||^2+E_h(u),

where Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. Therefore E_h(u)>=0 implies W(h)<=1/D.

## 1. An aggregate interpolation identity across the full tail

Let a prefix finish at y, with g=grad f(y), function value f_y, and certificate state(D,e,E_a). Take a bridge step tau>=0 to y'=y-tau g. Run a tail block from y' to z. Write its nonnegative proof weights as v, its mass as D_b, its gradient aggregate as U_b, and its intrinsic energy as E_b. Summing all Q_(i,y) over the tail, with weights v_i, gives exactly

  2D_b(f_y-f_z)
   =E_b+||U_b||^2+2(tau-1)<U_b,g>+D_b||g||^2
       +2sum_i v_i(-Q_(i,y)).                        (1)

To check this, substitute y-x_i=tau g+(y'-x_i) into the Q expression. The tail terms 2sum v_i(f_i-f_z)+sum v_i||g_i||^2+2sum v_i<g_i,y'-x_i> combine to E_b+||U_b||^2; the remaining cross terms are2(tau-1)<U_b,g> and D_b||g||^2. All interpolation terms on the right of(1) are nonnegative.

Unlike the adjacent-only update, this identity uses every tail iterate and retains the actual aggregate U_b.

## 2. Exact matrix transport with a primitive tail

Assume the prefix has the residual certificate

  E_a>=nu||e-K g||^2, nu>0.                         (2)

Let b be primitive with shifted physical capacity A=1+sum(b). Use its standard proof weights v=(b_0,...,b_(ell-1),A). Then

  D_b=2A-1,
  U_b=(y'-z)+Aq, q=grad f(z),
  E_b=V_b>=0,

where V_b=2sum b_i(f_i-f_z)+sum b_i(1-b_i)||g_i||^2-(A-1)A||q||^2 is precisely its primitive potential.

Increase the old terminal proof weight by eta and scale all tail weights by beta. Require old_terminal_weight+eta>=0 and beta>=0. The new weights have mass

  D'=D+eta+beta D_b,

and their residual satisfies

  e'-Aq=e+(eta-tau)g+(beta-1)U_b.                   (3)

Direct expansion of E for the joined block gives

  E'=E_a+beta E_b+2(D+eta)(f_y-f_z)
      +eta(1-eta)||g||^2-2eta<e,g>
      +beta(1-beta)||U_b||^2
      +2beta<U_b,(tau-eta)g-e>.                     (4)

Choose c=(D+eta)/D_b>=0 and nu'>=0. Substituting(1) into(4), and subtracting nu' times the squared residual in(3), gives the exact identity

  E'-nu'||e'-Aq||^2
  =E_a-nu||e-Kg||^2+(beta+c)E_b
     +2c sum_i v_i(-Q_(i,y))+(e,g,U_b)^T M(e,g,U_b),

where the symmetric3-by3 matrix is

  M_ee=nu-nu',
  M_eg=-nu K-eta-nu'(eta-tau),
  M_eU=-beta-nu'(beta-1),
  M_gg=nu K^2+D+2eta-eta^2-nu'(eta-tau)^2,
  M_gU=c(tau-1)+beta(tau-eta)-nu'(eta-tau)(beta-1),
  M_UU=c+beta(1-beta)-nu'(beta-1)^2.

Thus M>=0 suffices for another residual certificate, with new K'=A and mass D'. In particular it gives an actual objective bound1/D'. This algebra does not assume that D=2sum(a)+1.

## 3. A closed scalar subrule and an exact finite test

Keep nu'=nu fixed and set

  k=1+1/nu, beta=1/k, eta=(tau-K)/k.

The e row and column of M then vanish. The remaining matrix is

  [ D+((K-1)^2-(tau-1)^2)/k    c(tau-1) ]
  [ c(tau-1)                  c         ].

Put X=kD, r=tau-1, and X'=kD'=X+r+1-K+D_b. Subject to c>=0, this matrix is positive semidefinite exactly when

  r^2 X'<=D_b[X+(K-1)^2].                           (5)

For c>0 this is its Schur-complement condition after multiplication by kD_b. When c=0, X'=D_b and(5) is exactly the remaining diagonal condition. The state update is K'=A.

For a genuine finite example, use the prefix a=(5/3), weights u=(3/2,3/4), D=9/4, K=1, nu=27. Its identity is

  E_a=3(-Q_10)+27||e-g_1||^2.

Choose the primitive tail b=(sqrt(2)), whose capacity is A=1+sqrt(2), and use tau=1. The primitive tail certificate is explicitly

  V_b=2(1+sqrt(2))(-Q_10)+2(-Q_01),

with local tail indices. Then eta=0, beta=27/28 and the remaining transport matrix is diagonal with entries9/4 and(9/4)/(1+2sqrt(2)), both positive. Consequently the actual three-step schedule

  h=(5/3,1,sqrt(2))

has nonnegative proof weights (3/2,3/4,27sqrt(2)/28,27(1+sqrt(2))/28), and the certified bound

  W_3(h)<=14/(45+27sqrt(2)).

This is weaker than the exact ZJ benchmark. It tests the block rule but provides no improvement.

## 4. Balanced primitive-tail doubling cannot be competitive under this subrule

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), and start with any finite valid state of length1. At round j append a bridge and the standard silver primitive tail of the same length n_j=2^(j+1)-1, so n_(j+1)=2n_j+1. Its shifted capacity is A_j=lambda^(j+1), obtained by repeated ConPP using F(x,x)=lambda x. Use the fixed-nu subrule of part3 with any parameters satisfying its actual weight and PSD conditions.

For j>=1 the reset K_(j+1)=A_j gives K_j=lambda^j=A_j/lambda. Write X_j=kD_j. The recurrence and(5) become

  X_(j+1)=X_j+2A_j+r_j-K_j,
  r_j^2 X_(j+1)<=(2A_j-1)[X_j+(K_j-1)^2],
  X_(j+1)>=2A_j-1.                                 (6)

The last inequality follows from c>=0. Hence r_j<=sqrt(X_j+(K_j-1)^2), and since K_j>=1,

  X_(j+1)<=X_j+2A_j+sqrt(X_j).

Choose M sufficiently large that X_1<=M A_1 and(lambda-1)M>=2+sqrt(M). Induction gives X_j<=M A_j, so x_j=X_j/A_j is bounded. Let L=limsup_j x_j. If L=0 there is nothing to prove. Otherwise take a subsequence with x_(j+1)->L. Put t_j=r_j/A_j. From(6),

  lambda x_(j+1)=x_j+2-1/lambda+t_j,
  t_j^2 lambda x_(j+1)
    <=(2-1/A_j)[x_j/A_j+(1/lambda-1/A_j)^2].         (7)

Along a further subsequence x_j converges to a number at most L; thus t_j has a limit at least(lambda-1)L-2+1/lambda. Passing to the limit in(7) yields

  L*[(lambda-1)L-2+1/lambda]_+^2<=2/lambda^3.        (8)

Let L_0 be the unique solution, above(2-1/lambda)/(lambda-1), of the corresponding equality. The left side is strictly increasing on that interval, so L<=L_0. At L=sqrt(2), its value times lambda^3 equals lambda sqrt(2)=2+sqrt(2)>2; hence

  limsup_j D_j/lambda^(j+1)<=L_0/k<sqrt(2)/k.        (9)

The exact benchmark along these same dyadic horizons has liminf B_(n_j)/lambda^(j+1)>=2. A short scalar proof avoids importing any phase theorem. A complete balanced primitive tree with2^K unit leaves has capacity lambda^K. Replace one leaf by zero and collapse its parent using F(x,0)=x; the resulting legal tree has2^K-1 leaves. The map F is2-Lipschitz in each coordinate (differentiate its explicit formula, whose partial derivatives lie in[1,2]), so this single leaf replacement decreases the root by at most2^K. Thus P_(2^K-2)>=lambda^K-2^K. The admissible dominant split with that prefix and an empty tail gives

  B_(2^K-1)>=G(P_(2^K-2),1)>=2lambda^K-2^(K+1).

Since2/lambda<1, the claimed normalized lower limit follows. Combining with(9), the value1/D_j certified by this transport satisfies

  liminf_j (1/D_j)/U_(n_j)>=2k/L_0>sqrt(2)k>1.

Therefore this specific whole-tail rule, under the displayed constant-nu and equal-horizon recursion, cannot certify an improvement even over the basic primitive rate. This does not lower-bound W(h_j), does not exclude other parameter recursions, and does not show that all residual certificates fail.

## 5. What the next block interface must retain

The aggregate-only expression depends on(e,g,U_b) but not independently on q. Therefore a positive new residual coefficient forces K'=A unless another q-dependent certificate is retained: subtracting nu'||e'-K'q||^2 would otherwise give a negative q^2 coefficient in a variable absent from the original matrix. This explains the forced reset in the tested rule.

A source-supported extra piece is the primitive dual residual

  2(f_start-f_end)-2(A-1)||q||^2
     >=||g_start-(d_b+q)/A||^2.                     (10)

The proof in Grimmer–Shu–Wang arXiv:2410.16249, AppendixA.1 Lemma17, was read in full; before adding the optimum interpolation inequality it gives exactly(10), using the primitive potential plus nonnegative interpolation sums. That derivation uses the primitive potential itself and A=1+sum(b), not an assumed equality of primitive and dominant ambient classes. A future proof may rederive this combination explicitly rather than appeal to the final gradient-norm statement. No verifier promotion is claimed here. The next test is to retain both this dual residual and the objective residual through a join, with the bridge gradient and tail initial gradient distinct. A coarse sum of the bridge descent estimate and(10) has not recovered the basic large silver bridge, so it is not yet a replacement recursion.

# Second bounded seed-discovery phase

Status: completed at the explicitly authorized cumulative cap; no credible improvement found. This report adds to max4_bounded_seed_search_report.md and preserves all earlier evaluations. Numerical failure is not a nonexistence theorem.

Authorization: max4 TASK.md explicitly increased the cumulative cap from60 to600 evaluations while retaining the900-second solve/build cap. The persistent counter was not reset. Code is max4_primitive_pep.py and max4_primitive_phase2.py.

Sensitivity checks were performed before further local optimization, with ten counted evaluations at generic length3 and length7 points. Central differences used two step sizes,1e-4 and3e-5. The reported directional sensitivities agreed with finite differences with scaled errors between3.16e-6 and9.39e-6. One length7 plus probe had solver status optimal_inaccurate; both probes at the smaller step had optimal status. These are consistency checks at the tested nonsingular points, not a differentiability claim at all active-set changes. Full diagnostics are in max4_pep_sensitivity_checks.json.

The second phase then used530 additional full primitive PEP evaluations in seven local SLSQP runs. Each run had nonnegative independent step variables and fixed shifted sum1.002 times the relevant primitive DP mass. Starts included a previous near-DP sample, a descending arrangement, permuted DP blocks at lengths4 and7, and a nonuniform near-constant length5 block. No symmetry or recursive grammar was imposed. The objective and sensitivity were scaled by the square of the target mass to avoid excessively large initial local steps.

The two length3 searches reached the optimizer's normal numerical termination, after92 and49 evaluations respectively. Five other runs ended at their local evaluation quotas, including the final59-evaluation continuation of the length4 probe. Those truncated runs did not reach certified or numerical global optima. All statuses are retained in max4_pep_phase2_searches.jsonl.

Cumulative evaluations:600, including the original60, ten sensitivity probes, and530 new local evaluations. Recorded aggregate solve/build time:14.829684927 seconds. Each SDP used a single thread and at most an8-by8 Gram matrix. The persistent counter blocks further calls. Solver statuses across all600 evaluations:565 optimal and35 optimal_inaccurate; no result was promoted to a mathematical certificate.

At the fixed0.2% shifted-mass gain, the best sampled minimum energies were:
- n=3: -0.031038849256, evaluation162; normal termination was reached in this run.
- n=4: -0.034840056250, evaluation599; local quota reached.
- n=5: -0.535028447811, evaluation377; local quota reached.
- n=7: -0.046938039907, evaluation471; local quota reached.
All four best samples had solver status optimal. Their maximum interpolation residuals were below3.5e-11, their minimum Gram eigenvalues were positive, and their dual slack eigenvalue minima ranged from-2.44e-10 to-7.78e-9. The largest absolute duality gap among those four was3.55e-10. Thus they supply no credible near-zero feasibility candidate; these diagnostics still do not prove global failure of the seed class.

Across all600 evaluations, including inaccurate outputs, the largest positive interpolation residual was6.85e-8, the most negative slack eigenvalue was-6.54e-7, and the largest absolute duality gap was3.49e-6. Raw records, including every failed/inaccurate evaluation and candidate vector, remain in max4_pep_evaluations.jsonl. No counter reset, numerical-scan extension, new verifier submission, or worker restart was performed.

The exact reversed-seed Huber counterexample from the first phase remains a separate symbolic finding (81977b1b28582782); no further finite failure has been submitted for verification. The main target (P) is unresolved.

Next analytic mechanism: permit an objective certificate with independent weight sum D and a retained squared residual between its weighted gradient aggregate and the actual displacement/last gradient. This differs from finding a saturated primitive seed. Derive its exact finite algebraic update and test it on known extremizers before asserting any accelerated recursion or asymptotic advantage.

# Exact conditional amplification of a primitive seed

Status: complete unverified argument responding to master_guidance 56a30fdd80e74d9e. No seed meeting the hypothesis is known, and this is not a resolution of the unrestricted target. No additional verifier call is authorized.

## Claim and interfaces

Use the project's exact S_circle, S_bullet, B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n=1/B_n. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2) and rho=log_2(lambda). Suppose a nonnegative primitive schedule a of length r>=1 has shifted capacity A=1+sum(a) satisfying

    A>(r+1)^rho.                                      (1)

Then there are predetermined nonnegative schedules H_n of length (r+1)n, constructed solely with the usual ConPP/ConPD operations and repeated copies of a, such that

    W_((r+1)n)(H_n)
      <=[sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)]^(-2).                (2)

Moreover,

    liminf_(n->infinity) W_((r+1)n)(H_n)/U_((r+1)n)
      <=(r+1)^rho/A<1.                                (3)

Thus this stronger finite seed hypothesis is sufficient for the negative answer required by the project, along an infinite subsequence, without assuming convergence of B_n/n^rho or replacing the exact DP by a decimal profile.

The analytic schedule interfaces invoked here are the following standard Zhang–Jiang closure statements, stated to remove ambiguity about the hypotheses. At L=1 write Q_(i,star)=f_i-f_star-<g_i,x_i-x_star>+||g_i||^2/2. A schedule h with s=sum(h) is primitive if

  ||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_(i<n) h_i Q_(i,star)-2s(f_n-f_star)
  >=||x_n-x_star||^2+s(s+1)||g_n||^2

for every legal smooth convex GD trajectory. A schedule h is dominant if there are nonnegative weights u_0,...,u_n summing to 2s+1 such that

  ||x_0-x_star||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2(2s+1)(f_n-f_star)
  >=||x_0-x_star-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.

The empty schedule has both properties. If a,b are primitive, ConPP(a,b)=[a,phi(sum(a),sum(b)),b] is primitive. If a is primitive and b is dominant, ConPD(a,b)=[a,psi(sum(a),sum(b)),b] is dominant. Dominance implies W(h)<=1/(2sum(h)+1), since all Q_(i,star)<=0. Sources: Zhang–Jiang, arXiv:2410.12395, definitions and ConPP/ConPD theorems; the downloaded HTML numbers these Theorems11 and12 and the dominant objective implication Theorem6 (the published/new-paper numbering is3.1,3.2 and2.6). These closure proofs were read in the preceding max4 rounds; this new amplification argument does not assert their promotion to new project facts. Any future verification package must include their proper external-reference metadata or verified predecessors.

The only accepted project fact used below is 0be8b54e0a33381c, for the explicit scalar G join and its square-root nonexpansiveness. Other ingredients are proved here. The proof of the scalar power inequality is adapted, with notation changed, from Liu–Chen–Jiang–Wang arXiv:2609.11788v1, Lemma5.1 and AppendixC.1; its full proof is included below, so no phase theorem is assumed. Two Matlas queries, including the exact title/identifier, returned unrelated results; no Matlas grounding is falsely claimed.

## 1. A finite positive benchmark limsup

For shifted primitive masses x,y>=0 set

  F(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2.

Let q=1/rho. The inequality

  F(x,y)^q<=x^q+y^q                                  (4)

holds. Here is an elementary proof. Zero arguments give equality. For positive arguments write x=exp(m-t), y=exp(m+t), t>=0, using symmetry. Then

  F(x,y)=exp(m+arsinh(cosh(t))).

Inequality(4) is equivalent to J(t)>=0, where

  J(t)=q^(-1)log(2cosh(qt))-arsinh(cosh(t)).

Because 2^(1/q)=lambda, J(0)=0; direct exponential asymptotics give J(t)->0 as t->infinity. Its derivative is

  J'(t)=tanh(qt)-sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2).

Define alpha(t)=artanh(sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2)). Its derivative is cosh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2), strictly increasing from1/sqrt(2) to1. Also1/sqrt(2)<q<1: lambda>2 gives the upper inequality; lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16 gives q>3/4>1/sqrt(2). Hence alpha(t)-qt is strictly convex, vanishes at0, initially decreases, and tends to infinity. Its derivative increases to1-q>0, so it has exactly one positive zero t_0. Monotonicity of tanh implies J'>0 on(0,t_0) and J'<0 on(t_0,infinity). The two zero endpoint limits imply J(t)>0 for t>0, proving(4).

Write P_k=1+S_circle(k). Every primitive DP tree with k+1 leaves has root capacity at most(k+1)^rho by(4) and induction from unit leaves. Thus

  P_k<=(k+1)^rho.                                    (5)

The shifted dominant join is

  G(x,d)=2x+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8xd)/2.

Since sqrt(d^2+8xd)<=d+4x, one has G(x,d)<=d+4x. Unroll an optimizing dominant tree along its unique dominant continuation. If the attached primitive subtrees have leaf counts l_1,...,l_t, then each l_i>=1 and sum_i l_i=n. The final dominant leaf has mass1. By(5),

  B_n<=1+4sum_i l_i^rho<=1+4n^rho.                    (6)

The final inequality follows by repeated(a+b)^rho>=a^rho+b^rho for a,b>=0 and rho>1; for fixed a its difference has nonnegative derivative in b and vanishes at b=0.

Conversely, balanced primitive trees with2^k leaves attain shifted mass lambda^k, since F(x,x)=lambda x. For n=2^k the legal dominant split consisting of this(n-1)-step primitive prefix, one ConPD bridge, and an empty continuation gives

  B_n>=G(lambda^k,1)>=2lambda^k=2n^rho.              (7)

Consequently the real number

  K=limsup_(n->infinity) B_n/n^rho

exists in the extended-real sense and satisfies2<=K<=4. In particular it is finite and strictly positive. No single normalized limit or periodic-phase theorem is used.

## 2. Replace every primitive leaf; retain the terminal dominant leaf

For each n>=1 choose one fully expanded optimizing dominant DP tree T_n. It has n internal nodes and n+1 leaves. Exactly one leaf, at the end of its dominant continuation, has dominant type. All other n leaves have primitive type. This count follows inductively: at each dominant node the left subtree is a pure primitive tree and the right subtree contains exactly one dominant leaf; all-primitive nodes have two primitive children.

Replace each of the n primitive empty leaves by the certified schedule a. Keep the single dominant terminal leaf empty. Re-evaluate every bridge with phi or psi from its new child sums. This specifies a concrete nonnegative schedule H_n independent of the objective. Closure gives a primitive schedule at every primitive node and a dominant schedule at every dominant node. The original n bridge steps remain, and the inserted copies contribute rn steps. Therefore

  length(H_n)=(r+1)n.                                (8)

Positive homogeneity of F implies that every primitive subtree's mass is now exactly A times its old mass. Let D_n=2sum(H_n)+1. Along the dominant continuation compare the true construction with an auxiliary scalar evaluation having the same scaled primitive children but terminal scalar mass A instead of1. Homogeneity of G says that the auxiliary root mass is exactly A B_n.

For fixed x>=0 let

  T_x(z)=(z+sqrt(z^2+8x))/2.

Accepted fact0be8b54e0a33381c proves sqrt(G(x,d))=T_x(sqrt(d)) and that T_x is increasing and1-Lipschitz on nonnegative arguments. The true and auxiliary terminal square-root masses differ by sqrt(A)-1. Propagate both through the identical sequence of T_x maps. The difference remains between0 and sqrt(A)-1. Hence

  sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A B_n).    (9)

The lower bound is at least1 because B_n>=1. Dominance gives W(H_n)<=1/D_n, which proves(2). This handles the unmatched dominant leaf explicitly rather than scaling a nonexistent terminal schedule.

## 3. The input/output subsequences and exact comparison

Put m=r+1. Choose integers n_j->infinity with B_(n_j)/n_j^rho->K. From(9) and B_n=O(n^rho),

  D_(n_j)/n_j^rho->A K.

Indeed the difference A B_n-D_n is bounded above by2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A B_n), hence is O(n^(rho/2)) and becomes zero after division by n^rho. On the output horizons mn_j, the definition of the same global limsup gives

  limsup_j B_(mn_j)/(mn_j)^rho<=K.

Both statements concern the same input subsequence and its multiplied output subsequence. Their inequality directions therefore yield

  limsup_j W_(mn_j)(H_(n_j))/U_(mn_j)
  <=limsup_j B_(mn_j)/D_(n_j)
  <=m^rho/A.

This proves(3). Under(1), choosing delta=(1-m^rho/A)/2>0 ensures W_(mn_j)(H_(n_j))<=(1-delta)U_(mn_j) for all sufficiently large j. The lengths mn_j are unbounded, as required.

## What this does and does not change

The stronger seed score A/m^rho>1 is now accompanied by a complete amplification and exact-profile comparison. A seed merely exceeding P_r, but failing this stronger score, is not settled by this argument. No such above-power primitive seed was found in the authorized600-call experiment. The numerical cap remains exhausted; this conditional result does not authorize restarting that search. The current weighted-gradient residual approach addresses a different, nonsaturated certificate class and still lacks a competitive infinite recursion.

# A threshold-forced remote-cut requirement and an exact dense calibration family

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is authorized.
This is max4's round27 under master_guidance8211d6128deabdf3, following
completed baseline26 and one separately retained interrupted attempt.

The numerical launcher timed out during imports, before registering or returning
any case. The stale main-owned Meter lock and its ledger are preserved. The
incident is finding13d29bfe0c871924; no numerical restart is part of this paper.
Everything below is symbolic proof text, not a numerical candidate evaluation.

The accepted input is the original full certificate interface in
fact04219554e41d9750, Section10. Draft5bd01e2b19002a59 already contained the
adjacent certificate and weighted-cut identity; those are explicitly recalled
and rederived here, not claimed as new or imported as verified premises.
Draft443f4b4325a9901d already contained the positive flow/kernel center. The
new results are the uniform threshold consequence in Section3 and the explicit
dense strictly positive calibration family in Section5. No seed of sum168/25
is constructed, and no unrestricted no-seed theorem is asserted.

## 1. Original five-gradient space and its two exact balances

Let h=(h0,h1,h2,h3) have nonnegative entries. Put

    P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, 0<=i<=4; s=P_4; p_i=1+P_i.

Let e be the five-entry all-ones column, and let T have diagonal1,
T_it=h_t for t<i, and entries0 above the diagonal. Every formal gradient
g0,...,g4 is retained, even when a step vanishes. A full primitive certificate
has twenty nonnegative coefficients Lambda_ij, i!=j, zero diagonal, and

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),
    L^T e=omega, M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0.                 (1)

This is the exact interface in accepted04219554e41d9750. Its identity is

    V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g0,...,g4)>,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)
          +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t.

The matrix inequality in (1) is on ALL of R5. Since Te=p and
sum_(i<4)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2, direct expansion gives

    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2,
    e^T M e=0.

For any real column v, nonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v) for both signs
of sufficiently small t forces v^T Me=0. Therefore Me=0. Substituting
in (1) gives

    LP=diag(p)omega.                                    (2)

For an explicit component check at i<4,
(T^T omega)_i=-2h_i+2h_i(P_i+h_i), so
(Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2. At i=4 it is
-s(s+1)+(LP)_4/2. Thus every original row moment is retained.

Define mu_ij=Lambda_ij p_j. Equation(2), together with the value flow,
implies that mu is balanced at every vertex:

    sum_j mu_ij=sum_j mu_ji.                             (3)

Indeed (2) is equivalent to sum_j Lambda_ij p_j=p_i sum_j Lambda_ji.
No symmetry of Lambda or mu is assumed.

## 2. Weighted cuts, with all original edges present

For j in{0,1,2,3}, let z^(j) be1 on indices0,...,j and0 thereafter.
Write A_j=P_(j+1), and define nonnegative weighted crossing sums

    F_j=sum_(i<=j<k) Lambda_ik (p_k-p_(j+1)),
    B_j=sum_(k<=j<i) Lambda_ik (p_j-p_k).                 (4)

These are sums over every original crossing pair. Nonnegative h makes
every weight nonnegative; zero displacement simply has weight0.
The adjacent forward and backward edges have weight0.

Let O_j and I_j be the unweighted forward and backward crossing masses.
Summing the original value flows gives

    O_j-I_j=-2A_j.

Summing(3) over the prefix gives

    p_(j+1) O_j+F_j=p_j I_j-B_j,
    h_j O_j=2p_j A_j-F_j-B_j.                            (5)

We also have the full formal-direction identity

    (z^(j))^T M z^(j)=-A_j^2+O_j+F_j.                   (6)

To check it directly, Tz has entry1+P_i on i<=j and A_j otherwise.
The diagonal contribution is sum_(i<=j)h_i(2-h_i). The internal part
of z^T LTz is minus the internal row moment; its forward-crossing
part is sum_(i<=j<k)Lambda_ik(1+P_i-A_j). Sum the row moments(2)
over i<=j to replace the internal part by

    -2sum_(i<=j)h_i(1+P_i)
                  +sum_(i<=j<k)Lambda_ik(P_k-P_i).

The combined forward coefficient is1+P_k-A_j. The remaining scalar is

    sum_(i<=j)h_i(2-h_i)-2sum_(i<=j)h_i(1+P_i)=-A_j^2.

This proves(6) without deleting a gradient coordinate or an interpolation
pair. Define the step deficit polynomial

    Dstep_j=h_j(P_j+h_j)-2(P_j+1).                       (7)

Combining(5) and(6), for h_j>0, yields

    h_j (z^(j))^T M z^(j)
       =-A_j Dstep_j+(h_j-1)F_j-B_j.                    (8)

Consequently full PSD requires

    (h_j-1)F_j-B_j >= A_j Dstep_j.                      (9)

Formula(8) is the weighted-cut identity already present in
draft5bd01e2b19002a59, rederived here. The new point below is that a
UNIFORM strictly positive burden in(9) is forced at the target sum.

## 3. A uniform remote-edge burden at sum168/25

Claim. If a full certificate(1) has sum h>=168/25, then some
j in{0,1,2} has

    Dstep_j>1/16, h_j>1,
    (h_j-1)F_j-B_j > P_(j+1)/16,
    F_j>P_(j+1)/(16(h_j-1))>1/16.                       (10)

This holds for all nonnegative steps, with no assumption of positivity,
order, symmetry, rank, saturation, or support.

First prove a purely scalar assertion. Suppose, to the contrary, that
Dstep_j<=1/16 at all four indices. Let r(P) be the positive root of

    r^2+Pr=2(P+1)+1/16, P>=0.

The polynomial is strictly increasing in its nonnegative argument.
At argument2 its left side minus right side is2-1/16>0, so r(P)<2.
Implicit differentiation gives

    r'(P)=(2-r(P))/(2r(P)+P)>0.                          (11)

Thus h_j<=r(P_j). The following four rational comparisons now give
successive strict upper bounds, with no numerical root evaluation:

    h0<3/2, since (3/2)^2-2=1/4>1/16;
    h1<13/8, since P1<3/2 and
       (13/8)^2+(3/2)(13/8)-2(3/2+1)=5/64>1/16;
    h2<7/4, since P2<25/8 and
       (7/4)^2+(25/8)(7/4)-2(25/8+1)=9/32>1/16;
    h3<9/5, since P3<39/8 and
       (9/5)^2+(39/8)(9/5)-2(39/8+1)=53/200>1/16.

The use of the prefix upper bounds is justified by monotonicity(11).
Adding yields

    sum h<3/2+13/8+7/4+9/5=267/40<168/25,
    168/25-267/40=9/200.                                (12)

This contradicts the target sum. Some Dstep_j must therefore exceed1/16.
If h_j<=1, (7) gives Dstep_j<=P_j+1-2(P_j+1)<0. Hence this selected
h_j exceeds1, so the division used in(8) is legitimate even if another
step is0. Formula(9) proves the strict burden in(10).

At j=3, F_3=0 because the only forward destination is4 and its weight
p4-p4 is0. Since B_3>=0, (9) makes Dstep_3<=0 whenever h3>0.
The selected index cannot be3. Finally B_j>=0 and A_j>=h_j give
the last inequality in(10).

For direct use in the ORIGINAL twenty entries, the three alternatives
have these explicit weighted sums:

    F0=h1 Lambda02+(h1+h2)Lambda03+(h1+h2+h3)Lambda04,
    B0=0;

    F1=h2(Lambda03+Lambda13)
                      +(h2+h3)(Lambda04+Lambda14),
    B1=h0(Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40);

    F2=h3(Lambda04+Lambda14+Lambda24),
    B2=(h0+h1)(Lambda30+Lambda40)+h1(Lambda31+Lambda41).
                                                                  (13)

At least one of these three original-row combinations must satisfy(10).
In particular every target certificate needs a quantitatively nonzero
remote forward contribution. This does not cap how large that contribution
can be, and so does not exclude the full cone or assert that(10) suffices.

## 4. Recalled adjacent certificate, proved jointly

For strictly positive steps define an array supported only on adjacent
pairs by

    Lambda^a_(i,i+1)=2P_(i+1)p_i/h_i,
    Lambda^a_(i+1,i)=2P_(i+1)p_(i+1)/h_i.                (14)

Its net flow across cut i is-2P_(i+1), so all original value flows hold.
Its weighted adjacent masses are equal in the two directions after
multiplication by the destination p; hence(3), and then(2), hold.
In LT every entry outside the main diagonal and its two neighboring
diagonals vanishes: for a column earlier than i-1 the three row
contributions have the common factor h_t and their coefficients sum0;
for a column later than i+1 all contributions are0.
Its off-diagonal matrix entry on edge i is

    [-Lambda^a_(i,i+1)+(h_i-1)Lambda^a_(i+1,i)]/2=-w_i,
    w_i=(P_(i+1)/h_i)[2(P_i+1)-h_i(P_i+h_i)].             (15)

Because the row sums of M vanish, the whole original matrix is exactly

    M_a=sum_(i=0)^3 w_i(e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T.    (16)

The e_i here are the original standard coordinate columns in R5.
Thus w_i>=0 is sufficient for full PSD; if a w_i is negative, the
prefix indicator z^(i) gives negative energy in(16).
This family and its formula are prior research, not new discoveries.
Equation(12) shows why this adjacent family alone cannot reach the
present target, even allowing the small negative slack1/16 in(7).

## 5. An explicit full-support strictly positive calibration cube

Here is a NEW dense algebraic family useful for checking the ORIGINAL
and calibrated-K/skew numerical implementations. It is far below the
target threshold and is not proposed as an improving seed.

Choose arbitrary real steps in the whole cube

    1<=h_i<=17/16, i=0,1,2,3.                           (17)

Let Lambda^a be(14). Define

    mu=(P3+s)/2,
    v_i=2h_i/(mu-P_i), i<4; v4=2s/(s-mu); W=sum_i v_i,
    Lambda^c_ij=p_i v_i v_j/W for i!=j, diagonal0,
    epsilon=1/4096,
    Lambda=(1-epsilon)Lambda^a+epsilon Lambda^c.          (18)

Every denominator is positive because mu lies strictly between P3 and s.
These are rational functions of the four steps. All twenty entries of
Lambda are strictly positive, with the explicit uniform bound

    Lambda_ij>1/688128, i!=j.                            (19)

It has EXACT original flow and constant kernel, and its full matrix is
PSD. In the gradient-difference coordinates (g0-g4,...,g3-g4), its
four-by-four matrix K satisfies the explicit strict bound

    K >= (941/16384) I4.                                (20)

The following proves all of these assertions inside this one document.

First, sum_i v_i(P_i-mu)=0: the first four summands are-2h_i and the
last is2s. Hence sum_i v_i P_i=mu W and sum_i p_i v_i=(1+mu)W.
At node i the outgoing mass minus incoming mass of Lambda^c is

    p_i v_i-v_i sum_j p_j v_j/W=v_i(P_i-mu)=omega_i.

Its row moment sum_j Lambda^c_ij(P_j-P_i) equals p_i v_i(mu-P_i),
which is2h_i p_i at i<4 and-2s(1+s) at i=4. Thus it obeys exactly
the flow and kernel equations in Sections1--2. This is the center
construction from draft443f4b4325a9901d, now rederived. The convex
combination(18) preserves these equations.

For signs, s<=17/4 and h3>=1 imply

    8/17<=v_i<=17/4 for i<4,
    8/17<=v4<=17,
    W<=34<37.

The lower bound for i<4 uses mu-P_i<=s<=17/4; the upper bound uses
mu-P_i>=h3/2>=1/2. The terminal formula v4=4s/h3 gives the stated
bounds. Since p_i>=1, every off-diagonal center entry exceeds

    (8/17)^2/37=64/10693>1/168.

The last strict comparison is64*168=10752>10693. Since the adjacent
array is nonnegative, (19) follows by multiplication by epsilon.

It remains to prove full PSD quantitatively. In(15), P_(i+1)/h_i>=1,
2-h_i>0, and

    2(P_i+1)-h_i(P_i+h_i)
       =(2-h_i)P_i+2-h_i^2>=223/256.

Thus w_i>=223/256. For z in R4 extend it by z4=0 and set
d_i=z_i-z_(i+1). Since z_i=sum_(k=i)^3 d_k, the Cauchy inequality
gives ||z||^2<=10sum_i d_i^2. Consequently the leading block K_a of
(16) satisfies

    K_a >= (223/2560)I4 > (1/12)I4,                     (21)

where the last comparison is223*12=2676>2560.

For the center let u_i=p_i v_i and t_i=(T^T v)_i/W. For i<4,
0<u_i<=1139/64<18 and 0<=t_i<=17/16. The first bound uses
p_i<=67/16 and v_i<=17/4. The second uses
t_i=(v_i+h_i sum_(j>i)v_j)/W and h_i<=17/16.
Because the center Laplacian is diag(u)-u v^T/W, its leading block K_c
has exact entries

    (K_c)_ii=h_i(2-h_i)+u_i(1-t_i),
    2(K_c)_ij=u_j h_i-u_i t_j-u_j t_i, i<j<4.            (22)

Now 0<=h_i(2-h_i)<=1 and |1-t_i|<=1, so every diagonal has absolute
value at most19. Each off-diagonal has absolute value less than29,
using 3*18*(17/16)/2=459/16<29. Each absolute row sum is at most106.
For completeness, symmetry and 2|z_i z_j|<=z_i^2+z_j^2 imply directly

    |z^T K_c z|<=106||z||^2; hence K_c>=-106I4.          (23)

Combining(21) and(23) gives

    K=(1-epsilon)K_a+epsilon K_c
      >=[(4095/4096)/12-106/4096]I4
      =(941/16384)I4,

which proves(20). The whole matrix, not just its leading block, obeys

    M=[[K,-K1],[-1^T K,1^T K1]],

because Me=0. Its quadratic is therefore exactly the quadratic of K
on(g0-g4,...,g3-g4). Thus(20) proves full original-space PSD while
retaining all five formal gradients. It imposes no second kernel.

This gives a dense, strict, nonsaturated family with total sum at most17/4.
It is intended for implementation calibration and does not approach168/25.

## 6. Adversarial scope checks and the remaining task

The adjacent array alone is not treated as a lossless restriction:
Section3 explicitly shows that every threshold certificate needs a

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002651 -->
# Exact hostile test for the reset-doubling family

Status: self-contained unverified counterexample package. No further fact submission under the current allocation. It excludes every seed depth k >= 6 in the stated family, uniformly over subsequent doublings. It does not exclude the small remaining seed depths or all endpoint-potential constructions.

Use the notation of research/max4_reset_seed_boundary_certificate.md. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), sigma=1/lambda, A=lambda^k, B=beta_k-1, R=A+B+2. Then A=2B^2+B, and the actual seed is [pi^(k),B+1,1]. Let H_j=q^(k,j), with shifted sum R_j=lambda^j R, and H_(j+1)=[H_j,1+sigma R_j,H_j]. Every H_j ends with the same two steps (B+1,1). Its length satisfies len(H_j)+1=2^j(2^k+2).

For k>=6 we have A>=lambda^6=99+70sqrt(2)>197>171, hence B>9. In particular a=B/2>lambda. Define a one-dimensional smooth convex function by
f(x)=x^2/2 for -a<=x<=1;
f(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1;
f(x)=-a x-a^2/2 for x<=-a.
Its derivative clip(x,-a,1) is continuous, monotone, and 1-Lipschitz, and f(0)=0=min f.

Fix j>=0 and start H_(j+1) at x_0=R_j-B-2>0. The prefix of its first H_j before the last two steps has total sum R_j-B-3. All steps are nonnegative, so every sampled point of that prefix stays in the right affine region, and the prefix ends exactly at x=1. The (B+1)-step then reaches -B=-2a. The unit step reaches -a, with terminal gradient -a.

The following central step 1+sigma R_j sends -a to y=sigma R_j a. Since sigma a>1, we have y>R_j. The second H_j has total sum R_j-1. Every one of its iterates therefore stays in the right affine region with gradient1, and its final point is
x_N=R_j(sigma a-1)+1>1.
Consequently the exact normalized objective of this legal instance is

W_N(H_(j+1)) >= [R_j(B/lambda-2)+1]/(R_j-B-2)^2,
N+1=2^(j+1)(2^k+2).

This is a genuine lower bound for the proposed schedules; it is not an upper bound or a negative answer to (P).

Comparison with the exact benchmark, without decimal replacements:
Let P_m=S_circle(m)+1. The original recursion gives P_(2m+1)>=lambda P_m and P_(m+1)>=P_m. Thus P_(2^r-1)>=lambda^r. Since the dominant recurrence permits the split (n-1,0), B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1>=2P_(n-1)-1. Hence B_n>=2lambda^r-1 whenever n>=2^r. Here N>=2^(k+j+1), so B_N>=2lambda^(k+j+1)-1.

The displayed lower bound is strictly larger than (B/lambda-2)/R_j. Multiplication by B_N yields
W_N(H_(j+1))/U_N^ZJ > (A/R)(2B-4lambda) - (B/lambda-2)/(lambda^j R).
For B>9, A=2B^2+B>9(B+2), so A/R>9/10. Also lambda<5/2, hence 2B-4lambda>8. Finally,
(B/lambda-2)/(lambda^j R)<B/A=1/(2B+1)<1/19.
Therefore the ratio is greater than 36/5-1/19=679/95>7 for every k>=6 and j>=0.

The sharper asymptotic lower constant for each fixed seed depth is
liminf_(j->infinity) W_(len H_(j+1))(H_(j+1))/U_(len H_(j+1))^ZJ >= (A/R)(2B-4lambda).
It tends to infinity as k tends to infinity. Large reset seeds cannot be rescued by increasing the number of balanced doublings in this family.

Scope and next obligation:
This exact test does not close k<=5. In particular the previously interesting k=4 seed has B/2<lambda, so the central jump in this witness lands inside the region the second block can traverse. No upper certificate or successful recursion is known for that remaining case. A later candidate must either establish a concrete boundary-variable inequality for it or change the block rule; claiming that a unit step controls terminal gradients is invalid.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002652 -->
# The exact DP already dominates every reset-doubling candidate

Status: self-contained unverified route rejection. Uses only the universal Huber lower bound in accepted fact 0be8b54e0a33381c and elementary algebra of the original DP. No new verification allocation is requested.

For every k,j>=0, the reset-doubling schedule q^(k,j) from research/max4_reset_seed_boundary_certificate.md satisfies
W_(len q^(k,j))(q^(k,j)) > U_(len q^(k,j))^ZJ.
Thus the entire stated family fails as a direct negative construction, including the smaller seed depths left open by the asymmetric-Huber test. No assertion about other richer joins or a modified family is made.

Notation: lambda=1+sqrt(2), A=lambda^k, B=beta_k-1, R=A+B+2, A=2B^2+B. The length n and shifted sum of the candidate are
n+1=2^j(2^k+2), 1+sum q^(k,j)=lambda^j R.
Write P_n=S_circle(n)+1 and D_n=2S_bullet(n)+1 (D_n is the benchmark B_n, renamed here only to avoid conflict with the seed parameter B). The shifted joins are
F(X,Y)=(X+Y+sqrt(X^2+6XY+Y^2))/2,
G(X,D)=2X+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8XD)/2.
Both are increasing in their arguments. F(X,X)=lambda X.

First, D_n>=2P_n-1 for all n. To check the needed comparison, take X,Y>=1 and C=F(X,Y), so C^2-(X+Y)C-XY=0. The polynomial whose larger root is G(X,2Y-1) is
q(z)=z^2-(4X+2Y-1)z+4X^2.
Direct substitution gives
q(2C-1)=2[X-(2X+1)(C-X-Y)].
Here C-X-Y=XY/C. Moreover C<X+2Y<=Y(2X+1), since 2XY-X-Y>=0 for X,Y>=1. Hence q(2C-1)<0. Therefore G(X,2Y-1)>2F(X,Y)-1. Induct on n along a maximizing primitive split and use the increasing dominant join. The case n=0 is equality. This proves the claim without assuming that the dominant DP literally lists every primitive schedule.

Balanced primitive splits also give P_(2^r-1)>=lambda^r for each r>=0. For our n, use the legal dominant split
m=2^(k+j)-1, ell=2^(j+1)-1, n=m+ell+1.
It follows that
D_n >= G(lambda^(k+j),2lambda^(j+1)-1)
     = lambda^j G(A,2lambda-u), where u=lambda^(-j) in (0,1].

It remains to compare this with the candidate's Huber denominator 2lambda^j R-1. Set d=2lambda-u and c=8-2lambda-u. We have d>1, c>0, d-c=4lambda-8>0, and d+c=8-2u>0. Using A=2B^2+B gives the exact identity

d^2+8Ad-(4B+c)^2
=16(d-1)B^2+8(d-c)B+(d-c)(d+c)>0.

All quantities under comparison are positive. Taking square roots and substituting into G gives
G(A,2lambda-u)>2A+2B+4-u=2R-u.
Consequently D_n>2lambda^j R-1.

Finally the Huber lower bound from fact_id 0be8b54e0a33381c yields
W_n(q^(k,j)) >=1/(2lambda^j R-1)>1/D_n=U_n^ZJ.
This is a pointwise rejection for every member of the concrete family, so it is stronger than merely ruling out a fixed asymptotic improvement. The same sum argument applies to a permutation of any such schedule, but does not establish how to modify its steps successfully.

Relation to the requested spectral screening in master_guidance ac69664acf0dee30:
For a quadratic f_mu(x)=mu x^2/2, 0<mu<=1, the candidate has the exact scalar recursion
p_(j+1)(mu)=p_j(mu)^2 [1-(1+lambda^(-1)R_j)mu], R_j=lambda^j R,
p_0(mu)=p_pi^(k)(mu)(1-beta_k mu)(1-mu).
Equivalently,
p_j(mu)=p_0(mu)^(2^j) product_(r=0)^(j-1)[1-(1+lambda^(r-1)R)mu]^(2^(j-1-r)).
The vanishing at mu=1 does not control the rest of this interval. No full-interval stability theorem is claimed. Since the exact profile comparison above already rules out every member as a direct negative schedule, neither a numerical spectrum sweep nor a large boundary-certificate search is needed to make this route decision.

# Active candidate: reset seeds with retained boundary gradients

Status: exploratory plan, not an upper-bound theorem. The explicit hostile tests below forbid treating the seed as primitive or assuming arbitrary dominant seeds admit balanced doubling.

New active negative candidate: a unit-step reset of a silver objective block, followed by balanced sum-based doubling, with a richer certificate that retains the last two sampled gradients. This lies outside the accepted terminal-seed washout theorem and outside the weakened scalar-potential recursion. No upper bound for the recursive family is claimed. A simpler unreset variant is explicitly killed by a full-curvature quadratic at length 15, and the reset seed's primitive certificate is explicitly killed by an asymmetric Huber instance. Thus only a genuinely richer last-iterate objective certificate could make the active candidate work.

Fix lambda=1+sqrt(2) and sigma=lambda^(-1)=sqrt(2)-1. Let pi^(0) be empty and pi^(k+1)=[pi^(k),1+lambda^(k-1),pi^(k)]. These are the standard silver primitive blocks, of length 2^k-1 and shifted sum lambda^k, as follows algebraically from the exact ConPP recurrence.

Actual reset seed and candidate family:
beta_k=(3+sqrt(8lambda^k+1))/4,
q^(k,0)=[pi^(k),beta_k,1],
A_(k,0)=1+sum q^(k,0)=lambda^k+beta_k+1.
For j>=0 define
q^(k,j+1)=[q^(k,j),1+sigma A_(k,j),q^(k,j)],
A_(k,j+1)=lambda A_(k,j).
Lengths are n_(k,j)+1=2^j(2^k+2). Every step is predetermined and positive, and no extra state is used by GD. The final unit step in the seed makes the polynomial on f(x)=x^2/2 vanish, so every doubled schedule also vanishes on that quadratic. This is only one hostile-instance test, not a full-class guarantee.

Important failed propagation test:
Starting instead from h^(0)=[3/2] and applying the same balanced mass rule produces A_j=(5/2)lambda^j. If p_j is the final multiplier on f(x)=x^2/2 and gamma_j=A_j|p_j|, then gamma_0=5/4 and gamma_(j+1)=gamma_j^2, since sigma lambda=1. Hence gamma_j=(5/4)^(2^j). At j=3 the length is 15, A_3=(35+25sqrt(2))/2 lies strictly between 35 and 36, and gamma_3^2=(5/4)^16>32. Its normalized quadratic error is gamma_3^2/A_3^2>32/36^2=2/81, whereas the conjectured Huber-tight upper bound 1/(2A_3-1)<1/69. Thus balanced sum-based doubling of an arbitrary dominant seed is false, even if one guesses that an endpoint parameter shrinks. The reset changes a specific failing mechanism; it is not an assertion that all other obstructions disappear.

The reset seed itself is not primitive when lambda^k>3:
Write A=lambda^k, B=beta_k-1, so A=2B^2+B. Its shifted sum is R=A+B+2. For B>1 set a=B/2 and consider the asymmetric Huber function with derivative clip(x,-a,1) and minimum zero at x=0. Start at x_0=A. The whole silver prefix stays on the right affine ray with gradient 1, arriving at 1. The beta_k step arrives at -B. The final unit step arrives at -B+a=-a. At all sampled points Q_{i,star}=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2=0. At the final point g=-a and f=a^2/2, so the saturated primitive potential would require A>=Ra=RB/2. But
RB-2A=B(A+B+2)-2A=2B^2(B-1)>0.
Therefore the candidate must not be treated as a primitive seed. This exact failure does not rule out a weaker last-iterate objective certificate for the doubled family.

A tested richer certificate interface:
For a one-step schedule t in (0,3/2], put D=2t+1. Choose a nonnegative history weight u and final weight v=D-u. Define
J_(t,u)=2u(f_0-f_1)+u(1-u)||g_0||^2+2v(t-u)<g_0,g_1>+v(1-v)||g_1||^2.
With Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 and x_1=x_0-tg_0, direct coefficient comparison gives
J_(t,u)=(2D-2u)(-Q_10)+(2D-4u)(-Q_01)
+[-u^2+(4t-2)u-2(t-1)D]||g_0-g_1||^2.
Thus this is a genuine exact certificate whenever all three displayed coefficients and u,v are nonnegative. For t=1, u=3/2, it gives J=3(-Q_10)+(3/4)||g_0-g_1||^2. For t=3/2, u=2, it gives J=4(-Q_10).
The certificate implies the objective bound W_1(t)<=1/(2t+1), by adding u Q_(0,star)+v Q_(1,star) and completing the square ||x_0-x_star-u g_0-v g_1||^2. It retains information lost in a scalar endpoint-gradient coefficient.

The concrete finite algebra obligation for the reset candidate is to propagate a rank-one dominance certificate whose coefficient vector equals kappa times the steps in the interior but has independent coefficients on the last two sampled gradients and the final gradient. The original primitive-plus-beta_k objective block already has the explicit vector [pi^(k),A+1,2beta_k-1]. After the appended unit step, its bridge-gradient coefficient is of order A, whereas the physical bridge step is of order sqrt(A); this explains why a single common multiplier on all past steps loses necessary information. Derive the finite boundary-variable quadratic identity for composing two such objects; do not merely posit its feasibility. If closing the certificate class would require retaining every internal coefficient with no quantitative recursion, record that finite-state proposal as BLOCKED rather than presenting a restatement of full PEP as progress.

For a negative proof, a successful certificate still must yield a nonvanishing separation relative to the exact B_(n_(k,j))=2S_bullet(n_(k,j))+1, or must supply a way to insert the improved blocks into an objective construction with an exact profile comparison. The algebraic mass formula alone does neither. No new verifier submission is authorized for this exploratory package.

## DOC-002654 — project/research/max4_terminal_seed_washout.md

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\max4_terminal_seed_washout.md

原始字节数：6283；原始 SHA-256：c670ae96f212446aa2b67e7081405fe0e8a7eb96ce43f1babc9be1ad9c8ed9dd

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002654 -->
# Candidate: finite terminal seed washout

Status: verifier accepted; fact_id 0be8b54e0a33381c. This is not the project target.
Shared finding: 65e3a18284ee82c4.

## Statement

Finite terminal-block improvements wash out under the Zhang–Jiang ConPD rule.

Define phi(x,y)=(-x-y+sqrt((x+y+2)^2+4(x+1)(y+1)))/2 and psi(x,y)=(3-2y+sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9)))/4 for x,y>=0. Define S_circle(0)=S_bullet(0)=0 and, for n>=1,
S_circle(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+phi(S_circle(k),S_circle(n-k-1))+S_circle(n-k-1)],
S_bullet(n)=max_{0<=k<n}[S_circle(k)+psi(S_circle(k),S_bullet(n-k-1))+S_bullet(n-k-1)].
Write B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

For a nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite-dimensional convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, with x_{t+1}=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t)/L.

Let E be any nonempty finite set of finite-length nonnegative schedules, allowing the empty schedule. For b in E, write m_b for its length and s(b) for the sum of its entries. Define
C_E=max_{b in E} max{0,sqrt(2s(b)+1)-sqrt(B_{m_b})}.
Construct a schedule by choosing b in E and an integer r>=0, setting H_0=b, and, for j=1,...,r, choosing any nonnegative schedule a_j of length k_j>=0 with s(a_j)<=S_circle(k_j) and setting
H_j=[a_j, psi(s(a_j),s(H_{j-1})), H_{j-1}].
If N=m_b+sum_{j=1}^r(k_j+1), then every such schedule satisfies
W_N(H_r)>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2
=U_N^ZJ (1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2).
Moreover B_N>=2N+1. Consequently the lower-bound ratio tends to 1 uniformly over all schedules in this construction as N tends to infinity. In particular no fixed positive relative asymptotic improvement below U_N^ZJ can be obtained solely by using a fixed finite library of improved terminal blocks while retaining the displayed ConPD rule and the primitive-block sum caps. This is a statement about that construction class, not the unrestricted minimax problem.

## Proof

All arguments below are elementary and self-contained; no previously verified or external theorem is invoked.

1. A lower bound valid for every nonnegative schedule.
Fix any length n>=0 and any h in [0,infinity)^n. Put s=s(h)>=0 and tau=1/(2s+1). In dimension one, for any fixed L>0 define
f(z)=L z^2/2 when |z|<=tau, and f(z)=L(tau|z|-tau^2/2) when |z|>=tau.
The two expressions and their first derivatives agree at z=+tau and z=-tau. The derivative is L times z clipped to [-tau,tau]. This derivative is nondecreasing, so f is convex. Clipping is 1-Lipschitz: if u<=v, its increment lies between 0 and v-u, as can be checked from the three intervals separated by -tau and tau. Thus f has L-Lipschitz derivative. It is differentiable everywhere, has minimizer x_star=0, and f_star=0. Take x_0=1.
Nonnegativity of h implies every partial sum lies in [0,s]. An induction on i yields x_i=1-tau sum_{t=0}^{i-1}h_t: indeed this formula implies
x_i>=1-tau s=(s+1)/(2s+1)>=tau,
so f'(x_i)=L tau and the next gradient update gives the same formula at i+1. This also handles s=0 and equality at the boundary. Thus
x_n=(s+1)/(2s+1), f(x_n)=L tau x_n-L tau^2/2=L/(2(2s+1)).
Since |x_0-x_star|^2=1, this legal instance proves W_n(h)>=1/(2s(h)+1), including n=0.

2. Scalar form of one join.
For x,y>=0 put A=x+1>=1 and D=2y+1>=1. Direct substitution into the definition of psi gives
2[x+psi(x,y)+y]+1
=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2
=((sqrt(D)+sqrt(D+8A))/2)^2.
Denote the middle expression by G(A,D) and define T_A(q)=(q+sqrt(q^2+8A))/2 for q>=0. Then sqrt(G(A,D))=T_A(sqrt(D)). Both G and T are increasing in each of their displayed arguments.
For q>=p>=0, rationalizing the difference of square roots gives
0<=T_A(q)-T_A(p)
=(q-p)/2 [1+(q+p)/(sqrt(q^2+8A)+sqrt(p^2+8A))]
<=q-p,
because the denominator in the fraction is at least q+p; when q=p=0 the difference is zero without division. It follows also by interchanging q and p that T_A is 1-Lipschitz. In particular if q<=p+C for C>=0, monotonicity and this inequality imply T_A(q)<=T_A(p)+C.
All bridge steps are nonnegative: since x,y>=0, sqrt((2y+1)(2y+8x+9))>=2y+1, the formula gives psi(x,y)>=1.

3. A reference recursion dominated by the exact benchmark.
Fix a construction from the statement. Write N_0=m_b, N_j=N_{j-1}+k_j+1 and A_j=1+s(a_j). Its actual masses D_j=2s(H_j)+1 satisfy
D_0=2s(b)+1 and D_j=G(A_j,D_{j-1}).
Set reference masses E_0=B_{m_b} and E_j=G(A_j,E_{j-1}).
We prove E_j<=B_{N_j} by induction. The assertion is equality at j=0. If it holds at j-1, then the primitive-block sum cap and monotonicity of G give
E_j<=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).
In the defining maximum for S_bullet(N_j), choose the valid index k=k_j; the remaining index is N_j-k_j-1=N_{j-1}. By the identity in part 2 this choice yields
B_{N_j}>=G(1+S_circle(k_j), B_{N_{j-1}}).
This establishes the induction without any assumed structure for the a_j schedules beyond their length and sum cap.

4. Uniform comparison with a finite seed library.
By definition of C_E, sqrt(D_0)<=sqrt(E_0)+C_E. Applying the last assertion of part 2 inductively with A=A_j shows sqrt(D_j)<=sqrt(E_j)+C_E for every j. Part 3 gives
sqrt(2s(H_r)+1)=sqrt(D_r)<=sqrt(B_N)+C_E.
Apply the legal Huber lower bound in part 1:
W_N(H_r)>=1/D_r>=1/(sqrt(B_N)+C_E)^2.
This proves the claimed finite-horizon inequality.

5. Divergence and uniformity.
The maximum defining B_n includes k=0, so B_n>=G(1,B_{n-1}). Since sqrt(D^2+8D)>=D for D>=0, G(1,D)>=D+2. Starting with B_0=1 gives B_n>=2n+1 by induction.
Because E is finite, C_E is a finite constant independent of the depth r, of the chosen seed, and of all the a_j schedules. Thus
(1+C_E/sqrt(B_N))^(-2)>=(1+C_E/sqrt(2N+1))^(-2)->1.
Explicitly, for epsilon in (0,1), when C_E>0 choose any integer N_epsilon>=0 for which
sqrt(2N_epsilon+1)>=C_E/((1-epsilon)^(-1/2)-1);
if C_E=0 take N_epsilon=0. Then for every constructed H_r of any length N>=N_epsilon,
W_N(H_r)>=(1-epsilon)U_N^ZJ.
This threshold is independent of the constructed schedule. Were a fixed delta in (0,1) and an unbounded sequence of constructed schedules to satisfy W_N(H_r)<=(1-delta)U_N^ZJ, choose epsilon=delta/2 and N>=N_epsilon, obtaining a contradiction. The conclusion is restricted to the displayed construction; it imposes no assertion on arbitrary schedules outside that class.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002654 -->

## DOC-002655 — project/research/max4_ternary_cut_capacity.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002655 -->
# Exact optimality screen for a three-copy, two-bridge primitive join

Status: complete unverified construction-class obstruction, not a statement about unrestricted minimax optimality. No fact submission under the current allocation.

A concrete candidate was
c=2/3, d=(5sqrt(7)-11)/6, M=3+c+d=(11+5sqrt(7))/6,
J(a)=[a,1+cA,a,1+dA,a], A=1+sum a.
It has cdM=1 and M>F(1,1+sqrt(2)), where F is the exact shifted primitive join. Thus it passes the full-curvature product balance and suggests a larger primitive capacity than two ordinary binary joins. It is nevertheless invalid. The general argument below screens all positive c,d in this motif and proves that none can exceed the existing binary factor when the seed is quadratic-balanced.

Definitions. At L=1 write x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i, g_i=grad f(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). For a nonnegative schedule h with sum s=R-1 define its saturated intrinsic primitive energy
V_h=2 sum_(i<n) h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sR||g_n||^2.
The primitive property means V_h>=0 on every smooth convex trajectory. The following necessary tests are explicit legal one-dimensional functions, so no conjectural composition theorem is used.

1. An exact cut constraint for every primitive schedule.
Choose any step b>=1, let P be the sum of preceding steps, and T the sum of following steps. Set a_minus=(b-1)/(T+1). Use f'(x)=clip(x,-a_minus,1), f(0)=0, and x_0=P+1. Its prefix stays on the positive affine ray and arrives at1. The b-step arrives at -(b-1). All tail iterates stay on the negative affine ray, and the final point is -a_minus. This follows from nonnegative partial sums and (T+1)a_minus=b-1. The degenerate b=1 case is harmless.
At each sample, Q_(i,star)=f_i-g_i x_i+g_i^2/2=0, since the function is quadratic in its core and affine with the matching offset on each tail. A direct expansion using the GD recursion yields
V_h=x_0^2-x_n^2-2s f_n-sR g_n^2+2 sum_i h_i Q_(i,star).
Thus this trajectory gives V_h=(P+1)^2-R^2 a_minus^2. If h is primitive it follows that
(b-1)R <= (P+1)(T+1).                                      (CUT)
This is a necessary primitive-energy inequality, not merely the objective single-cut lower bound.

2. Full-curvature condition.
For f(x)=x^2/2 and x_0=1, all Q_(i,star)=0, and V_h=1-R^2 product_i(1-h_i)^2. Hence a primitive schedule must obey
R |product_i(1-h_i)| <=1.                                  (QUAD)

3. The three-copy motif cannot exceed the ordinary two-join factor.
Let a be any nonnegative block with shifted sum A>0 and |product_i(1-a_i)|=1/A. This includes each standard quadratic-balanced basic primitive block. Form J(a) with c,d>0 and put M=3+c+d, so its shifted sum is R=MA.
The two bridge instances of (CUT) give
Mc<=2+d, Md<=2+c.
Condition (QUAD) gives Mcd<=1, because the endpoint quadratic multiplier has magnitude cd/A.
Writing t=c+d=M-3 and u=(M-1)/(M+1), the cut conditions are exactly c,d<=u. If M<=4 there is already no gain over F(1,1+sqrt(2))>4. Suppose M>4. Both c,d lie in [t-u,u]. At a fixed sum t, the product c(t-c) is concave, so
cd >= u(t-u)=(M-1)(M^2-3M-2)/(M+1)^2.
Combining this with Mcd<=1 gives
M^4-4M^3-1<=0.
This polynomial is strictly increasing for M>3, and has one root M_*>4. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2) and C=F(1,lambda). Its defining equation C^2-(1+lambda)C-lambda=0 gives lambda=C(C-1)/(C+1). Substitution into lambda^2-2lambda-1=0 gives C^4-4C^3-1=0. Therefore M_*=F(1,lambda), and necessarily
M<=F(1,1+sqrt(2)).
The existing two-step binary parenthesizations of three copies attain this scalar factor. This argument rules out a larger primitive factor for the displayed three-copy motif, regardless of a proposed richer certificate.

4. Exact hostile instance for the displayed new constants.
Take a=[sqrt(2)], A=lambda, c=2/3 and d=(5sqrt(7)-11)/6. One has 0<d<2/3. Use f'(x)=clip(x,-1/4,1), f(0)=0, and x_0=A. The first sqrt(2)-step reaches1; the first bridge reaches -cA. Every remaining step stays on the negative affine ray because their total is T=(2+d)A-1 and
-cA+T/4 <= -1/4 iff d<=2/3.
The endpoint is x_5=-cA+[(2+d)A-1]/4. On the negative affine ray, the primitive terminal expression x_5^2+2s f_5+sR g_5^2 equals (x_5-s/4)^2. Since s=MA-1,
x_5-s/4=-A[c+(1+c)/4]=-(13/12)A.
Thus V_J=A^2-(13A/12)^2=-(25/144)A^2<0. This is a direct failure of the proposed primitive certificate in dimension one. It does not rely on reversing schedules or on a general H-duality assertion.

Search record and scope.
Matlas returned unrelated statements for the requested primitive/H-duality composition search. The downloaded GSW paper arXiv:2410.16249 was checked: its Section 3.1 states H-duality for basic schedules, so no unsupported universal reversal closure was assumed. A tiny SDP check was considered, but cvxpy, numpy, scipy, clarabel, and scs are absent; no numerical solver, exhaustive search, or installation was run. The exact cut construction made one unnecessary.

The obstruction uses the seed's exact quadratic balance and the three-copy/two-bridge form. It does not close non-balanced seeds, independently chosen blocks, four-block joins, or a direct objective certificate. Those would require their own feasible certificate and exact-profile comparison. Any proposed reopening must identify which hypothesis changes and retain the cut test as a mandatory hostile example.
